AboutThisGuide
About This Guide
Description
The Designer  User Guide describes the Designer  application and explains how to use it to configure 
Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics (Opcenter EX MDD) or Opcenter Execution Core 
(Opcenter EX CR) to fit your factory model.
Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR must already be successfully installed and the factory model 
completed to use Designer. Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Installation 
Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core Installation Guide. 
Audience
This guide is intended for technical personnel responsible for implementing Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter 
EX CR and using Designer, and for Siemens business partners who implement Opcenter EX MDD or 
Opcenter EX CR. 
Conventions Used
For Code Samples
Code samples are provided according to the development platform supported by the product. This guide 
uses the following font convention to depict programming code:
 
' Delete the service
Set Assoc = Nothing
ServLink.RemoveService("AService")
Important:Siemens provides sample code as is without warranty of any kind, either express or 
implied, including but not limited to the implied warranties of merchantability and/or 
fitness for a particular purpose.
Because of the limitation of the physical page, a line of code meant to occupy one line in the program 
wraps to the next line in the documentation. If this occurs, the wrapped lines are indented. For example:
 
Set Assoc = Nothing ServLink.RemoveService("AService");
Set Assoc = New ServLink.AddService
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide xix

AboutThisGuide
For Specific Information Types
This guide uses these formatting conventions.
Convention Definition Example
Blue underlined text Indicates a hyperlink, and applies only 
to URLs and e-mail addresses
www.siemens.com
Bold font Indicates icon names, buttons, user 
entries, and field names
Click Associate on the toolbar.
Enter a value in the Item Caption field.
Italic font Indicates the result of a step in a 
procedure
Refer to the Opcenter Execution Core 
Modeling User Guide.
Click New. The Workflow page appears 
in the Modeling page.  
Important: Indicates that the omission or result of a certain action can cause minor 
errors, but does not necessarily cause data loss and might require repeating a 
procedure or changing settings
Note:  Indicates relevant information that reinforces an idea and is not critical to any 
action that you must perform
Documentation Release Number
The plus sign (+) in the documentation release number indicates the document is compatible with 
software Release 2410 and later releases. If the content of the document is updated for a subsequent main 
release such as Release 2504 or 2510, the documentation release number will be updated.
How This Guide is Organized
This guide is organized as follows.
Chapter / Appendix Contents
Chapter 1, Introduction Provides an overview of the Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR 
architecture and discusses object model concepts.
Chapter 2, Module Licensing Explains how to gain authorized access to separately purchased 
features.
Chapter 3, Getting Started Describes and illustrates the Designer user interface.
Chapter 4, Configurable Data 
Objects
Explains how to create CDOs, subclass named data objects, and 
perform related tasks. Describes and illustrates the CDO 
Definition window.
Chapter 5, Automated Steps in 
Defining a CDO Subclass
Explains the automated steps used to define a CDO subclass.
xx DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 21 ===
AboutThisGuide
Chapter / Appendix Contents
Chapter 6, Configurable Logic 
Flows
Explains how to add and configure CLFs. Discusses the 
underlying parameters and variables. Describes and illustrates 
the Logic Flow Definition window.
Chapter 7, Fields Discusses field types and data types and explains how to work 
with CDO fields. Describes and illustrates the Field Types 
Definition window.
Chapter 8, Tables Discusses the capture and storage of persistent data in a 
relational form. Describes the table types. Explains when to 
create, modify, and delete tables. Describes and illustrates the 
Table Definition window.
Chapter 9, Functions Describes functions that have been written to perform a wide 
variety of tasks such as simple arithmetic, searching complex 
data structures, database queries, report writing, data collection, 
and data validation.
Chapter 10, Queries Discusses the use of Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR 
queries. Describes and illustrates the Query Definition window. 
Chapter 11, Labels Discusses the purpose of Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR 
labels. Describes and illustrates the Label Definition window, and 
explains how to add, modify, and remove a label. 
Chapter 12, Categories Explains the types of new storage and table categories. Describes 
and illustrates the Category Definition window, and explains 
how to add a new category.
Chapter 13, Filtering on 
Organizations and Roles
Provides guidance for retrieving a list of organizations or roles to 
which an employee has access and retrieving a list of employees 
for a particular organization/role combination.
Chapter 14, Importing Application 
Metadata
Explains how to import Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR 
metadata to synchronize two databases.
Appendix A, Valid Characters and 
Reserved Words
Identifies a list of valid characters that can be used in field names 
and CDO names. Also identifies the words to avoid in field 
names and CDO names and fields in Designer.
Appendix B, Unified Expressions Describes the syntax used in Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX 
CR unified expressions and the contexts in which they apply. 
Appendix C, Utilities Describes the external utilities available for managing metadata.
Related Publications
This guide is part of the Opcenter Execution Core documentation set. Refer to these Siemens publications 
to supplement information found in this guide:
• Opcenter Execution Core Installation Guide
• Opcenter Execution Core Modeling User Guide
• Opcenter Execution Core System Administration Guide

Security Information
Siemens provides products and solutions with industrial security functions that support the secure 
operation of plants, systems, machines and networks. 
In order to protect plants, systems, machines and networks against cyber threats, it is necessary to 
implement – and continuously maintain – a holistic, state-of-the-art industrial security concept. Siemens’ 
products and solutions constitute one element of such a concept. 
Customers are responsible for preventing unauthorized access to their plants, systems, machines and 
networks. Such systems, machines and components should only be connected to an enterprise network or 
the internet if and to the extent such a connection is necessary and only when appropriate security 
measures (e.g. firewalls and/or network segmentation) are in place. 
For additional information on industrial security measures that may be implemented, please visit 
https://www.siemens.com/industrialsecurity. 
Siemens’ products and solutions undergo continuous development to make them more secure. Siemens 
strongly recommends that product updates are applied as soon as they are available and that the latest 
product versions are used. Use of product versions that are no longer supported, and failure to apply the 
latest updates may increase customer’s exposure to cyber threats. 
To stay informed about product updates, subscribe to the Siemens Industrial Security RSS Feed under 
https://www.siemens.com/industrialsecurity.

Chapter 1: Introduction
Overview
Both Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics (OC EX MDD) and Opcenter Execution Core (OC 
EX CR) are built on a model-driven architecture that enables the graphical configuration of entities and 
their behavior in the system. This allows the system to be extended and modified without changing the 
source code.
In This Chapter
This section provides a basic view of the Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR architecture and discusses 
object concepts relevant to configuring the system. 
This chapter contains these topics:
• Architecture
• Modifying the Object Model
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 1-1

=== PDF PAGE 26 ===
Chapter1:Introduction
Architecture
The pillars of the model-driven  architecture are the entities and behaviors. The entities define business 
objects, and the behaviors that define business logic are referred to as:
• Configurable Data Objects (CDOs)
• Configurable Logic Flows (CLFs)
The CDOs and CLFs build the Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR object model.
Object Model and the Factory Model
The object model defines the attributes of an object, for example, the data elements of a product. The 
factory model defines the values of the data elements, for example, the instance of a particular product 
type. Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Modeling User Guide or the 
Opcenter Execution Core Modeling User Guide for information about the factory model.
Designer
The  base product includes definitions to meet the general requirements for any manufacturing business. 
Siemens provides additional products to extend the object model by incorporating domain-specific CDOs 
and CLFs.
The extensions to the model-driven architecture are implemented in Designer, an integral part of the base 
product. This allows you and Siemens’s services and development teams to adapt the object model to the 
specific needs of your business. Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics 
Installation Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core Installation Guide for information on Designer.
Designer Databases
The object model definitions are in the Microsoft Access databases (InSite.mdb and SiteInfo.mdb), also 
referred to as the metadata databases. Most of the configurations are written to InSite.mdb, while a small 
portion devoted to site-specific definitions are stored in SiteInfo.mdb. These databases are portable and 
can be maintained in Designer on any machine running the Microsoft Windows operating system. The 
definitions are released to the production database and activated using Management Studio. 
Metadata Reports
You can generate Metadata reports using a batch file that is provided with Designer:
C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\InSite Administration
1-2 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 27 ===
Chapter1:Introduction
This diagram shows the process.
The first step in extending the object model is to understand the CDOs and CLFs included with your 
Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR version and edition. Then you are able to identify the additional 
entities required to run your business requirements.
Upon completion of designing and activating your object model, you are be able to use the Portal to define 
your factory model.
Configurable Data Objects
The business objects are referred to as Configurable Data Objects (CDOs). Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter 
EX CR distinguishes two types of CDOs:
• Data CDOs
• Service CDOs
Data CDOs and Service CDOs consist of a set of data elements called fields. A field is of a specific data type 
defined in the system and can be:
• a simple data type like string, integer, decimal
• a complex data type like object
Furthermore, a field can be scalar or a list. 
Data CDOs
Data CDOs define the entities in your system and their behavior in the form of CLFs. The entities in the 
system represent business objects such as:
• Product
• Workflow
• Equipment
• Material
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 1-3

=== PDF PAGE 28 ===
Chapter1:Introduction
The Data CDOs are persistent objects in the system. Their data is non-volatile and lives beyond system 
restarts. The entirety of Data CDOs define the state of the system. Service CDOs and CLFs are used to alter 
the state of an object.
Service CDOs
Service CDOs define the tasks or units of work you can perform on Data CDOs. The Service CDOs include 
objects like:
• Product Maintenance (to add product definition)
• Move (to move a part to a downstream workflow step)
• Collect Data (to capture quality and parametric data)
The behavior of these services is defined in the form of CLFs. In contrast to Data CDOs, Service CDOs are 
not persistent, but alter the persistent state of the system by operating on Data CDOs.
The unit of work performed by a Service CDO will always be executed as a transaction under the ACID 
principle (atomicity, consistency, isolation, and durability). This means that either all alterations to the 
system state will get committed as a whole or no alterations will be committed.
This diagram illustrates the interaction between a data CDO and a Service CDO.
Fields
A field, whether it belongs to a Data CDO or Service CDO, is of a data type predefined in Opcenter EX MDD 
or Opcenter EX CR. Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR support the following field data types:
• Boolean (true or false)
• Decimal (fixed point number type with precision and scale)
• Fixed (fixed point number type)
• Float (approximate number type)
• Integer (whole number type)
• String (text)
• Timestamp (date and time)
1-4 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 29 ===
Chapter1:Introduction
• Object (CDO)
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR support scalar fields and list fields. Following the model-driven 
architecture of Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR, a field can have CLFs attached to define custom 
behavior in response to events in the system, like retrieving the field's value.
Logic
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR logic are defined by CLFs. A CLF consists of functions and defines 
the sequence in which these functions are executed at system runtime. A CLF can be attached to a Service 
CDO, Data CDO, and CDO field.
A CLF definition can perform many different actions in the system, such as:
• Determine the next workflow step
• Validate the user input to a field
• Calculate the quantity of a container
The role of functions in the system is to provide sufficiently granular, reusable components to build 
various types of CLFs.
This diagram shows the conceptual architecture of the model-driven architecture and the relationships of 
Service CDOs, Data CDOs, fields, CLFs, and functions.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 1-5

=== PDF PAGE 30 ===
Chapter1:Introduction
Modifying the Object Model
The Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR object model consists of data and service CDOs, CLFs, functions, 
and other definitions. Designer allows you to modify and extend the object model. Any objects that you 
have modified since the last time you saved are displayed in green.
Inheritance
Inheritance is the concept of deriving an object type from an existing one, hence creating a subclass to the 
object type. The subclass inherits the data elements and behavior of the object type. This concept not only 
reduces the time to configure the system to your specific business model needs; it also ensures an 
inherent validity of the defined subclass.
Creating a Subclass
The recommended approach to customizing the Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR object model is to 
create subclasses of default object types and then customize the subclasses to meet your requirements. 
The Designer application allows you access to the fields of the subclass and their properties as well as to 
the CLFs attached to the subclass. You can add, remove, or modify fields, attach or detach CLFs, and 
change function parameters.
Subentities
The process of adding fields to a subclass may result in an object type with a large number of fields. 
Instead of directly adding your fields to the subclass, you can choose to add your fields to an object type 
that is an integral part of the subclass you are trying to extend. In Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR, 
these integral object types are referred to as Subentity CDOs. The relationship between one of your 
subclasses and the integral Subentity object type is established by adding a field of the Subentity object 
type to your subclass.
Refer to  “Configurable Data Objects” for information.
1-6 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

Chapter 2: Module Licensing
Introduction
Siemens offers separately licensed modules to customers to meet industry-specific needs. These modules 
are available, at an additional cost, to customers who purchase Opcenter Execution Medical Device and 
Diagnostics (Opcenter EX MDD) or Opcenter Execution Core (Opcenter EX CR).
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR customers have the option to purchase a license for  separately 
licensed modules. Refer to the Release Matrix for Opcenter EX MDD on the Siemens Support Center or the 
Release Matrix for Opcenter EX CR on the  Siemens Support Center for a complete list of included and 
optional features.
Licensing provides you with authorized access to these modules.
This chapter provides information regarding how licensing is incorporated after you have purchased a 
license for one or more modules.
In This Chapter 
This chapter contains these topics:
• Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR
• License Management
• Concurrent Licensing
• Designer
• Feature Entitlement Tab
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 2-1

=== PDF PAGE 32 ===
Chapter2:ModuleLicensing
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR
Both Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR include the industry’s most robust Manufacturing Execution 
System, as well as the Process Design & Modeling capability that allows you to define, enforce, and audit 
your manufacturing process. 
For example, base Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR include:
• Portal for Modeling and Manufacturing Execution
• Designer to configure the application to fit your factory model
• Portal Studio for customizing and maintaining the Portal
• A Manufacturing Audit Trail that provides the complete history of all manufactured lots, batches, 
and serialized units, spanning production in multiple plants
• Data Collection
Separately Licensed Modules
In addition to base Manufacturing, Siemens offers several separately licensed modules that deliver 
additional capabilities to customers in several different industries. One or more of these modules can be 
purchased by customers who have already purchased base Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR.
All separately licensed modules are disabled in Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR. Refer to the Release 
Matrix for Opcenter EX MDD on the Siemens Support Center or the Release Matrix for Opcenter EX CR on 
the  Siemens Support Center for a complete list of included and optional features.  
Enabling Modules
There are two ways to install separately licensed modules: 
• At the initial installation.
If you purchased one or more separately licensed modules, then use the correct license file and 
serial number for that particular combination of Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR and mod-
ules during installation.
• After either Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR is already installed.
You must obtain a new license and serial number for the module from Siemens.
Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Installation Guide or the Opcenter 
Execution Core Installation Guide  for complete installation information.
2-2 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 33 ===
Chapter2:ModuleLicensing
License Management
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR contain the base product. It also includes individual products and 
modules you can license separately. You need a valid license file and serial number to install Opcenter EX 
MDD or Opcenter EX CR. You must also install the Siemens License Server using the  Siemens Licensing 
application.
The license file is generated based on the products and modules you licensed when you purchased 
Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR. There is one license file. You need the license file to install the 
Siemens License Server.
The serial number is the key you enter when installing Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR. It determines 
the products and modules available for  installation. Your unique serial number includes  any additional 
products or modules you purchased.  If you do not purchase any additional modules, then all entities 
related to these modules will appear red in Designer. 
Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Installation Guide or the Opcenter 
Execution Core Installation Guide for information on licensing.
 License Server
Designer utilizes the  Siemens License Server. A new Windows service is created after you install the server. 
Designer requires the license server. 
License Key File
The license file contains the runtime license for the server and the Designer.  The license server validates 
you are entitled to use separately licensed modules. If the license server is not available, you will be unable 
to use these features.
Note: You can upgrade an existing Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR installation to include addi-
tional modules if you have a new license file and new serial number. Refer to the Opcenter Exe-
cution Medical Device and Diagnostics Installation Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core 
Installation Guide  for information.
Licensing affects how different entities perform when selected in Designer. The Designer topic in the next 
section describes the behavior of entities with feature licensing installed.
Note: A red icon next to an entity in Designer indicates the module is not licensed or the license 
server is not available. A yellow icon indicates a separately licensed module that has been prop-
erly licensed.
Updating License Keys
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR allow you to update a production license without having to take 
the server down. You can generate a temporary license in event of emergency; for example, if you are 
locked out of of Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR due to expiration. Refer to the Opcenter Execution 
Medical Device and Diagnostics Installation Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core Installation Guide for 
information.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 2-3

=== PDF PAGE 34 ===
Chapter2:ModuleLicensing
Follow these steps to update the license keys:
1. Generate a temporary  license file.
2. Replace the license file with the temporary file on the license server  using the licensing 
application.
Note: This does not disturb the running security server or the application server. The security 
server will use the new license information when it performs its periodic license refresh 
and expiration check.
2-4 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 35 ===
Chapter2:ModuleLicensing
Concurrent Licensing
Customers purchase a finite number of licenses from Siemens when they purchase Opcenter EX MDD or 
Opcenter EX CR. The number of users concurrently logged into the Portal cannot exceed the number of 
licenses purchased.
When someone logs into the Portal a session is started and one license is considered in use for that specific 
user account.
Once the number of users logged in equals the number of licenses available (purchased), no new users 
will be allowed to log in until a license becomes available (when another user closes a session, for 
example).
Anyone who attempts to log in once the license limit has been reached will receive an error stating there 
are no available licenses.
Once users are logged in, they can access all licensed features and modules. Therefore, concurrent 
licensing is different than feature licensing, which determines what modules have been licensed.
Note: If your company needs more concurrent licenses than what you currently own, please contact 
Product Support to purchase additional licenses.

The Feature Entitlement tab is available for CDOs, CLFs, and Functions. There are two different views of

membership for the entity. Newly added CLFs and Functions default to the default feature set associated 
with the corresponding workspace. You can select an alternate entitled feature when adding a new entity. 
The Feature Membership field only provides features that are entitled in the current active workspace.

Opcenter Execution Core System Administration Guide for information on entitling features in your 
highest active workspace.

=== PDF PAGE 46 ===
Chapter3:GettingStarted
Logging In and Starting Designer
Siemens provides three default employee logins: CamstarAdmin, Administrator, and InSiteAdmin. These 
users must have a valid Windows account  if you want to use one of them as the default login to Designer.
Before Logging In to Designer
Access to Designer is based on Windows security. Ensure Designer users have a valid Windows account 
and that they have been added as Designer users in Management Studio. Refer to the Opcenter Execution 
Medical Device and Diagnostics System Administration Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core System 
Administration Guide  for information on adding users to Designer.
The Siemens License Server must  be installed and running, and  the Security Services must be running. 
Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Installation Guide or the Opcenter 
Execution Core Installation Guide for information on starting services.
When Logging In and Starting Designer
The Designer Login window defaults to the name used to log on to the server the first time you log in to 
Designer. The Designer Login window defaults to the previous Designer user name and password for 
future log in attempts.
Designer does not require a user to enter a password when the Designer user name is the same as the user 
name used to log on to the server. Designer requires a user to enter a password when the Designer user 
name is different from the Windows logon user name.
An error message appears preventing you from logging into Designer if  CSI is the highest active 
workspace. You must activate another workspace in Management Studio and then log in to Designer. 
Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics System Administration Guide or the 
Opcenter Execution Core System Administration Guide  for information on activating workspaces.
How to Log into Designer
Follow these steps to log into Designer:
1. Click Start > Opcenter Execution MDD> Designer. The Designer Login window appears.
Or
Click Start > Opcenter Execution Core> Designer. The Designer Login window appears.
2. Keep the current User Name, or enter an authorized Designer user name.
3. Enter your Password.
4. Verify the paths to the MDBs. If the databases were moved to a different directory, enter those 
paths. 
5. Click Login. The main Designer window appears.

Designer Header

and description of the highest active workspace.
The highest active workspace code and description displays in the brackets in the Designer header before 
the path to the InSite.mdb. The workspace code and description signals which workspace receives the 
updates made in Designer. For example, you could expect to see "Designer [Workspace [200 - Customer]]," 
indicating that any updates  in Designer will be applied to the corresponding  workspace with a workspace 
code of 200 and a description of Customer.
The highest active workspace is set in Management Studio. Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical 
Device and Diagnostics System Administration Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core System 
Administration Guide for information.

This  table describes the toolbar button options.
Button Click this button to . . .
CDOs Display the CDO Definition window where you create and modify configurable data 
objects to meet your factory model needs. Use it to define an object and associated 
items such as fields, field properties, tables, and maps. The exposed CDOs are based 
upon the View setting: Basic or Advanced.
CLFs Display the Logic Flow Definitions window that displays configurable logic flows (CLFs) 
by category. CLFs specify an object’s behavior and provide a list of functions that 
perform one or more tasks. Use it to view, add, modify, remove, or disable CLFs.
Fields Display the Field Type Definitions window that displays all of the supported field types.

by category.

add, modify, or remove user-defined functions.

any predefined queries you create. Use it to view predefined queries and to create and 
update queries to be used by the transaction logic.
Labels Display the Labels window, which lists all system and user labels. You can customize all 
text (for example, comments and user interface labels). 
Categories Display the Category Definition window. Use it to define table and storage categories. 
Table categories are used to group tables. The categories are used only for 
organizational purposes. Storage categories are used to store a reference to a table 
category, to indicate if the CDO is nonpersistent and does not require storage, or if the 
storage Category is to be determined later.
Help Display the online Help in a browser window. Help appears in the preferred language 
set in your browser if translated help is available. Help appears in English by default.
Update DB Update the transaction and DataStore databases. A confirmation dialog box appears 
indicating that the application will save the current MDB file and update the database 
according to the settings in the Preferences dialog box. If you select Yes, a status bar 
appears at the bottom of the window to indicate the update  is in progress.

Selection Tree
The selection tree contains a list of choices available for the selected toolbar option. Click the plus sign to 
expand and expose more options. Details about the selection appear on the right pane.
Searching for Objects
Enter the first few characters or the entire object name in the filter field to search for a specific object. 
Entering a string displays an alphabetical list of objects with names beginning with that string. 
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 3-7

=== PDF PAGE 52 ===
Chapter3:GettingStarted
This image shows an example of all CDOs beginning with "Data."
Select the object you want from the list or enter the entire object name and click Go. The application 
expands the selection tree and highlights the selected object.
Command Buttons
Command buttons are located at the bottom of the selection tree and are not available with the Labels 
menu bar or toolbar options.
• Back  - returns you to the previously selected object.
• Add - adds custom CDOs, CLFs,  fields, tables, functions, queries, and categories.
• Rename - changes the name of user-defined CDOs, CLFs, fields, tables,  functions, and queries.
• Remove - removes user-defined CDOs, CLFs, fields, tables,  functions, queries, and categories.
Command buttons on the right pane are specific to the displayed definition window: CDOs and CLFs are 
described in the topics about those options.
3-8 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 53 ===
Chapter3:GettingStarted
Shortcut Menus
Shortcut menus are available in selected areas when you right-click your mouse. Double-clicking on 
selected areas display additional windows for property settings, or allows you to move to an area being 
referenced by the current selection. These are documented in topics throughout the guide. 
Opening and Closing Designer Definition Windows
A definition window opens by default if an option on the toolbar is clicked. If another button is clicked, a 
definition window opens and remains in focus without closing the previous window. Multiple definition 
windows can be open, and you can arrange the layout of the windows through the Window option on the 
menu bar.
To close any definition window, click the X button. The main Designer window has another X button on 
the topmost right that terminates the Designer application.
Saving Database Changes
You can save changes to the database by selecting Save on the File menu. Select Save As to save changes 
with a different MDB name.
How to Exit Designer
To exit Designer, either select Exit on the File menu, or click the X in the upper right corner of the Designer 
window. A confirmation dialog box appears  if you try to exit Designer without saving your changes.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 3-9

=== PDF PAGE 54 ===
Chapter3:GettingStarted
Applying Designer Changes and Maintaining Designer 
Users
Changes made to the factory model using Designer are recorded in the InSite.mdb, a Microsoft Access file. 
Following that process, you need to propagate the changes to the transaction database, Siteinfo.mdb, and 
if applicable, to the DataStore database.
Applying Designer Changes
Each time you make changes using Designer, you must apply these changes to your transaction and 
DataStore databases. You do this by:
• Clicking the Update DB button in the toolbar. 
Or
• Performing the Update Database task in Management Studio. 
Changes are applied to the highest active workspace set in Management Studio. The Designer header 
displays a workspace code and description indicating which workspace changes are being applied to. Refer 
to "Designer Header" for information.
Utilizing Mass Updates
You can utilize a mass update setup for performing transactions on large sets of data, such as when 
processing a large number of containers or one large batch transaction. Contact Product Support via  
Support Center for assistance configuring Designer to utilize mass updates.
For more information
Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics System Administration Guide or the 
Opcenter Execution Core System Administration Guide  for more detailed information .
Preferences Dialog Box, DB Update Tab
The application saves the current MDB file and updates the database according to the settings in the 
Preferences dialog box when you click the Update DB button. A confirmation dialog box appears indicating 
that the application will save the current MDB file and update the database according to the settings in the 
Preferences dialog box. You access the Preferences dialog box in the File menu.

Chapter3:GettingStarted
Managing Workspace Overrides
Workspaces provide the ability for customers, partners, and Siemens to change or customize Opcenter 
Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics (Opcenter EX MDD) and Opcenter Execution Core (Opcenter EX 
CR) without overwriting changes made by others. Making changes in a workspace isolates those changes 
from any updates made to any other workspace. For example, changes made in the highest level 
workspace do not affect the base application workspace. Similarly, if a customer accepts an update to the 
base application workspace, customizations in their workspace are not overwritten.
Siemens provides four types of workspaces:
• Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR base
• Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR industry solution
• Partner
• Customer
The active workspace with the highest sequence number takes priority and is where all work and user 
content is saved. The customer workspace is the highest priority workspace. You cannot deactivate a 
workspace after working in the workspace in Designer.
A default color is associated with each workspace. You can change the default color in Management 
Studio.          It is recommended that you specify light colors so that the colors have better visibility in Designer. 
This table lists the default colors for each workspace.

When Managing Workspace Overrides
Designer works with the highest active workspace as set in Management Studio. Designer uses visual 
indicators to identify objects that have been overridden by workspace updates. The type of indicator that 
appears depends on how the object was overridden.
Designer uses these rules when managing workspace overrides:
• Color highlighting appears on the name of the object when the object was overridden by another 
workspace. The color of the highlighting indicates the workspace in which the object was 
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 3-15

=== PDF PAGE 60 ===
Chapter3:GettingStarted
overridden. CDO Maps and CDO Field Maps appear bold and do not use a color highlighting visual 
indicator.
• The background color of overridden CDOs  turns to the color associated with the worskpace in 
which the CDO was overridden.
• Objects that you override in Designer in your own workspace appear in bold text. The values that 
are overridden will be highlighted using the workspace color after your select the object.
• The field name displays in bold if it has been overridden on the Fields tab. The 
Overridden/Defined In column shows the workspace code for the workspace in which the field 
was overridden, for example, MoveIn (Workspace - 100).
• Field overrides at the lowest level appear as new fields,  not overrides.
• Fields defined in a CDO (not inherited) that are overridden in another workspace at the same 
level do not appear as  overrides.
• Tooltips do not appear on disabled fields.
Note: Background colors on objects overridden in other workspaces are hidden by default. You must 
set Display Only My Workspace Overrides to Off in the View menu to display highlighting for 
objects overidden in other workspaces. Refer to "Main Designer Window" for information.

Chapter 4: Configurable Data Objects
Introduction
Configurable data objects (CDOs) are Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR objects that can be configured 
to meet your factory model needs. CDOs represent Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR data. 
Note: Siemens recommends prefixing customer-specific CDOs to avoid conversion issues during 
future Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR version upgrades. This recommendation extends to 
Siemens service consultants, Siemens third-party integrators, and customer personnel. 
In This Chapter
This chapter contains these topics:
• Data and Service CDOs
• CDO Definition Window
• CDO Tabs
• Adding CDOs
• Finding and Removing CDOs
• Events and Methods
• Events
• Methods
• Events and Methods Procedures
• Defining a Container CDO Subclass
• Defining a NamedDataObject Subclass
• Defining a RevisionedObject Subclass
• Defining a Subentity CDO Subclass
• Defining a Service CDO Subclass
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 4-1

=== PDF PAGE 64 ===
Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
Data and Service CDOs
The Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR object model is based on standard object-oriented concepts 
and practices. CDO definitions are synonymous with classes. Objects derived from the BaseObject are its 
subclasses. Each BaseObject subclass can also have its own subclass. The typical way to customize the 
Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR structure is to define CDOs and add fields. There are two categories 
of CDOs: Data and Service.
Data CDOs
Data CDOs define the entities in your system and their behavior in the form of CLFs. Data CDOs include:
• Product
• Workflow
• Equipment
• Material
Data CDOs are persistent objects in the system; its data is non-volatile and continues beyond system 
restarts. The entirety of data CDOs define the state of the system. Service CDOs and CLFs are used to alter 
the state of an object.
Service CDOs
Service CDOs define the tasks or unit of work you can perform on data CDOs. Service CDOs include: 
• Product Maintenance (to add product definition)
• Move (to move a part to a downstream workflow step)
• Collect Data (to capture quality and parametric data)
CLFs define the behavior of service CDOs. Service CDOs are not persistent, but alter the persistent state of 
the system by operating on data CDOs.
The unit of work performed by a service CDO is executed as a transaction under the ACID principle 
(Atomicity, Consistency, Isolation, and Durability). This means that either all alterations to the system state 
get committed as a whole or none get committed.
4-2 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 65 ===
Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
This diagram shows the interaction between a data CDO and a service CDO.
Fields
Data and service CDOs are predefined in Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR, and support these field 
types:
• Boolean (True or False)
• Decimal (fixed point number type with precision and scale)
• Fixed (fixed point number type)
• Float (approximate number type)
• Integer (whole number type)
• String (text)
• Timestamp (date and time)
• Object (CDO)
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR also support scalar fields and list fields. Following the model-driven 
architecture, a field can have CLFs attached to define custom behavior in response to events in the system, 
like retrieving the field's value.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 4-3

=== PDF PAGE 66 ===
Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
CDO Definition Window
The process of configuring or adding CDOs is performed in the CDO Definition window. The structure of 
the classes is listed in the left window pane when you click the CDO button. The first level objects are 
under the BaseObject. 
Click the CDOs button on the toolbar to open the CDO Definition window. The leftmost pane contains a 
selection tree that lists all the available CDOs. The topmost CDO, BaseObject and the Fields tab are the 
default selections displayed.
The Back button enables you to navigate back through your history of CDO selections.
CDO Shortcut Menu
Right-click a CDO to display the CDO shortcut menu. These tasks are available on the shortcut menu:
• Add Subclass
• Paste field–A field copied from the Fields tab of any object can be pasted into another object.
User-defined Menu Options
The disabled options are enabled after you derive and select your own subclass. You cannot modify default 
CDOs, but you can modify your own subclasses. These options are enabled on the shortcut menu:
• Rename a CDO Definition
• Add Associated Service CDO–enabled only for user-defined NDOs, ROs, and Subentities
• Remove CDO Definition
• Go to Database Table Definition–enabled only for persistent CDOs, default or user defined 
(BaseObject is not persistent, but the Container CDO is). Select the option to go to the database 
table definition for the selected persistent CDO. 
Since BaseObject is a default CDO, other options are disabled.
Additional Menu Options

If you check Details and select an event or method, the list of associated access modes is displayed. The

checks for the access mode associated with the method to determine whether it exists in the service 
permissions on any role that the user is granted. If it has not been granted, the service will fail.

=== PDF PAGE 86 ===
Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
Abstract Flag
If a CDO is flagged as Abstract, the application server enforces this by not allowing the creation of an 
instance, even if the client UI requests that an instance be created.  The Portal also understands this 
setting.
Some base classes are defined as abstract in Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR, but this setting is 
overridden in the subclasses
The following table provides an example.
CDO Abstract Instantiate the data object
NamedDataObjectMaint ✓                                  No
EmployeeMaint   Yes
You are not expected to create instances of NamedDataObject because it is too generic, but you can 
instantiate container levels.
Client UI Flag
The Client UI setting exposes or hides the services under Maintenance. You can use the default modeling 
menu initially for the   Portal. If you decide you do not need some of the objects on the menu, you can 
change the Client UI setting. You do not make the change directly to the object, however; you change the 
object’s corresponding Maint object.
Affect on Inheritance
The inheritance of Abstract and Client UI settings affects the client application as follows:
• Using the example of NamedDataObjectMaint, while you cannot instantiate it because it is 
abstract, it needs to be exposed; otherwise, you will not see its subclasses and therefore cannot 
maintain any named data objects. 
• Not exposing a class to the UI means you cannot access it, even if that class is not abstract.
The following example table expands on the previous example.
CDO Abstract Exposed Can you instantiate the 
data object?
NamedDataObjectMaint✓                                  ✓                                  No
EmployeeMaint   ✓                                  Yes
UserCodeMaint ✓                                  ✓                                  No
BillTypeMaint   ✓                                  Yes

=== PDF PAGE 87 ===
Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
Adding CDOs
Create or add a CDO by clicking the Add button below the selection tree or by right-clicking in the selection 
tree pane and selecting Add Subclass.
When Adding CDOs
Workspace prefixes  are automatically inserted  before the name of an item when adding a new item, if a 
prefix has been assigned to the highest active workspace. Workspace prefixes  indicate which workspace 
Designer is applying changes to.  For example, the prefix "C200"  would be associated to the workspace 
with a prefix set as "C200" the highest active workspace in Management Studio. Siemens recommends you 
use the prefix supplied by  Designer when adding new items.
You can assign or modify workspace prefixes in Management Studio.  Refer to the Opcenter Execution 
Medical Device and Diagnostics System Administration Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core System 
Administration Guide  for information.
Note: The base application workspace (csi) cannot have a workspace prefix assigned to it.
When Adding a Subclass
Siemens recommends using a unique name when adding a CDO subclass. If the CDO and CDO subclass 
share the same name, and the subclassed CDO's instances are saved in the parent CDO’s table in the 
database, then no instances will appear for selection during Modeling export (even when defined for both 
the parent and child CDOs). 
If the CDO and CDO subclass have different names but are saved to the same table, all instances defined 
for both the parent and child CDOs are returned, regardless of whether you choose the parent or child 
CDO during Modeling export. The name of the subclassed CDO appears as the Object Type during a 
Modeling export.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 4-25

Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
Finding and Removing CDOs
The following procedures explain how to quickly find a specific CDO by entering search criteria, and how 
to remove a user-defined CDO subclass.
You can remove only CDO subclasses that are user-defined. If you create subclasses under subclasses, you 
can remove them by starting from the bottom level. The following list summarizes the steps for removing 
the generic CDO, but you should refer to each exercise in this chapter for detailed steps on removing 
specific types of CDOs.
How to Find a CDO
Follow these steps to find a CDO:
1. Open Designer.
2. Click CDOs. The CDO Definition window appears.
3. Enter the first few characters of the CDO name in the filter field above the selection tree. A list of 
objects containing the characters displays.
4. Locate and select the CDO you want from the list. The application expands the selection tree,  
highlights the selected CDO, and displays the related CDO Definition window.
How to Remove a CDO
Follow these steps to remove a user-defined CDO:
1. Open Designer.
2. Click CDOs. The CDO Definition window appears.
3. Select the user-defined CDO to be removed.
Note: The Remove button is disabled if you select a default CDO.
4. If the Remove button is disabled for a user-defined CDO, click the Where Used tab and find the 
CDOs that reference the CDO you want to remove and clear any field overrides. Refer to “Creating 
Field Overrides” for information.
Note: When you have cleared all references to the CDO you are removing, the Remove button 
will be enabled.
5. Click Remove, located below the CDO selection tree in the left pane. The CDO is removed.
6. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 4-29

=== PDF PAGE 92 ===
Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
Events and Methods
A CDO initiates a CLF using either an event or a method. Events and methods act as pointers from CDOs to 
a CLF. Events and methods are defined as follows:
• Event
An event is a delegate that may contain a reference to a CLF.
CDOs and Fields have a predefined set of events that are automatically triggered by the system. 
For example, when a client application sets a value on a CDO field, the system triggers the Val-
idate event, among others. The set of events cannot be extended by the user. 
The system behavior can be modified by attaching CLFs to events, modifying function parameter 
values of associated CLFs, or detaching CLFs from events. 
• Method
A method is a delegate that contains a reference to a CLF.
CDOs defined by Siemens commonly have methods attached. These methods are triggered by the 
logic flow of a service, a CLF, an event, or directly from a client application. The set of methods 
attached to a CDO can be extended by the user. A method is created by attaching a CLF, removed 
by detaching a CLF, and altered by modifying function parameter values. The creation of methods 
is a common means to partition complex CLFs into reusable components that are executed 
sequentially.
Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Technical Reference: Client APIs 
or the  Opcenter Execution Core Technical Reference: Client APIs for information on using 
Siemens' .NET Client APIs.
Note: An event or method on a CDO can only be associated with one CLF. This allows each type 
of CDO to respond to the same event in a unique way. 
4-30 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

If a CLF is called or triggered, each function is called in order, one at a time.

=== PDF PAGE 98 ===
Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
Methods
Users can create methods by calling out a CLF and attaching it to a new event. Siemens attached five 
methods without any preexisting logic to some events in shop floor transactions to make it easier for users 
to customize these transactions. The methods, also called user exits, are:
1. PreValidate_User
This method is called by the BeforeValidate event. You can insert additional processing logic that 
occurs after default execution logic.
2. Validate_User
This method is called by the AfterValidate event. You can insert additional processing logic that 
occurs after the required value validations.
3. PreExecute_User
This method is called by the BeforeExecute event. You can insert additional processing logic that 
occurs before default transaction execution logic.
4. Execute_User
This method is called by the BeforeExecute event. You can insert additional processing logic that 
occurs after default transaction execution logic.
5. ExportInfo_Initialize_User
This method inherited and is called by the BeforeExecute event. You can insert logic related to the 
creation or initialization of additional ExportInfo objects.
6. ProcessAfterCommit_User
This method is called by the AfterCommit event. You can insert additional processing logic that 
occurs after default transaction post-commit logic.
Custom Logic Examples
You will most likely customize a transaction under ShopFloor by first adding a subclass and then some 
user-defined fields. Your subclass will then inherit ShopFloor events. Some examples of how you can 
customize logic are:
• You can attach a CLF to the Validate_User event to validate the new fields or you can attach a CLF 
and map the new fields in some CDOs to the Execute_User event. 
• You can also make calls to your COM-compliant custom executions. 
Limitation
There is a possibility that the transaction you are extending might fail. For example, you might be using a 
variable in the Transaction scope that is already in use by Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR.
How to View the User Exits
Follow these steps to view user exits:
1. Click CDOs on the Designer toolbar.
4-36 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 99 ===
Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
2. Select the ShopFloor CDO.
3. Go to the Events tab and click Include inherited. Inherited events and methods are added to the 
display.
4. Scroll down to the methods section, and locate the five user exits.
5. View an example of a system event calling one of the methods as follows:
a. Expand the BeforeValidate event.
b. Expand the attached CLF, ShopFloor::Call.
c. Expand the Call function.
The function is calling the PreValidate method. If you want to add your own logic to the BeforeVal-
idate event, select the PreValidate method. This method calls three more methods: PreValidate_
User, PreValidate_Specific, and PreValidate_Generic. 
The call tree further leads to more methods being called, each one becoming specific. The last 
method being called provides a placeholder function, _Comments, to which you insert your logic. 
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 4-37

=== PDF PAGE 100 ===
Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
Events and Methods Procedures
You can attach a CLF to an event or create a new method. Only one CLF can be active for each event or 
method. You can override an existing CLF that is inherited from your current CDO. 
If a CLF is attached to an event or method at your current CDO level, you must detach that CLF before you 
can attach another one.
Viewing Events and Methods
Events are marked with lightning bolt icons. This image shows events and methods on the Events tab. 
How to View the Events and Methods Attached to a CDO
Follow these steps to view the events and methods attached to a CDO: 
1. Open Designer.
2. Click CDOs on the toolbar. The CDO Definition window appears.
3. Select the CDO for which you will view the associated events. For example, select Container.
4. Click the Events tab. The events and methods for the selected CDO are displayed:
Note: For base classes like Container, the methods defined at that class are displayed. But if 
you selected subclasses, the inherited methods are displayed only if you click Include 
inherited.
5. Click Show Variables to view the variables defined for the CDO’s methods and CLFs.

How to Attach a CLF to an Event or Method 
Follow these steps to attach a CLF to an event or method:
1. Open Designer.
2. Click CDOs on the toolbar. The CDO Definition window appears.
3. Select a user-defined CDO.
4. Click the Events tab.
5. Check the Include Inherited check box to view inherited events and methods.
6. Select the event or method to which you want to attach a CLF. The Attach CLF button is enabled.
7. Click Attach CLF. The Find Definition window appears, displaying the library of available CLFs. 
High Performance CLFs are available for selection if you are licensed for this feature.
Note: You can also right-click on the event or method and select Attach CLF.
8. Expand the CLF category containing the needed CLF.
9. Select the desired CLF. 
10. Click Select.
11. Click on the event or method you just modified to save your changes.
12. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.
13. Optionally, to view which CLF is active and which ones are overridden, click to open the event or 
method to which the CLF was added. The new CLF appears with a CLF icon next to it. 
Note: The former CLF appears with an icon 
  next to it, indicating that it is overridden. You can 
have a stack of overridden CLFs; the last CLF on the list is always the active CLF.
Note: You can reattach a CLF from the context menu instead of detaching then attaching the CLF.

How to Detach a CLF From an Event or Method
This procedure detaches a CLF previously attached, but keeps it in the CLF library, making the CLF available 
anytime. You cannot detach an inherited CLF.
Follow these steps to detach a CLF from an event or method:
1. Open Designer.
2. Click CDOs on the toolbar. The CDO Definition window appears.
3. Click the Events tab.
4. Select the desired CDO. The events and methods associated with the selected CDO appear.
5. Check the Include Inherited check box to view inherited events and methods.
6. Click to open the event or method from which you will detach the CLF.
7. Select the CLF to be detached.
8. Click Detach CLF or right-click on the CLF and select Detach CLF. A confirmation dialog box 
opens, asking you if you are sure you want to detach this CLF.
Note: If the Detach CLF button is not enabled, the CLF is inherited and can only be overridden.
9. Click Yes. The CLF is removed from the event or method. The overridden CLF that was 
immediately above the removed CLF becomes active (the 
  icon is removed).
10. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.

Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
How to Add a Method to a CDO
CDOs typically inherit all of the methods necessary to perform the desired task. However, some cases 
might require additional methods. When you add a method to a CDO, you are also adding a CLF that 
comes with the new method. The new method defaults to the name of the attached CLF. You can rename 
the method for your own use.
Follow these steps to add a method to a CDO:
1. Open Designer.
2. Click CDOs on the toolbar. The CDO Definition window appears.
3. Click the Events tab.
4. Select the CDO.
5. Check the Include Inherited check box to view inherited events and methods.
6. Click the New Method button, or right-click on the list of events and select New Method. The 
Find Definition window appears.
Note: The Find Definition window provides you a list of CLFs. High Performance CLFs are 
available for selection if you are licensed for this feature. The CLF will be added as a 
method and given the default name of the selected CLF. 
7. Expand the CLF category containing the desired CLF and select that CLF.
8. Click Select. The new method appears at the bottom of the user-defined methods. 
9. Rename your method if needed, as follows: 
a. Select the new method.
b. Pause, then click on the new method again to place it in rename mode. 
c. Enter the new name.
d. Press Enter to save your entry. The method is now available to be used.
10. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.
How to Remove a Method From a CDO
Follow these steps to remove a method from a CDO:
1. Open Designer.
2. Click CDOs on the toolbar. The CDO Definition window appears.
3. Click the Events tab.
4. Select the CDO.
5. Check the Include Inherited check box to view inherited events and methods.
6. Expand the method to be removed. The attached CLF is displayed. A stack of CLFs might be 
attached, but only the last one is active.
7. Select the active CLF attached to the method. (If you select the method name instead of the 
attached active CLF, the Detach CLF button remains disabled.)
8. Click Detach CLF. A confirmation dialog box appears, asking if you are sure you want to detach 
the CLF.

9. Click Yes. The last overridden CLF becomes active.
10. Repeat steps 7-9 as many times as necessary to remove a stack of overridden CLFs. The method is

11. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.

Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
Defining a Container CDO Subclass
Container CDO subclasses contain the definition for the  Container, the basic tracking unit. It refers to the 
lot or batch of products in your factory model. The Container is identified by a name and a level. This topic 
explains the steps required to define a Container CDO subclass. Most of the steps are automated.
When Defining a Container CDO Subclass
You can also extend the Container CDO that has a more sophisticated data structure, such as a subentity 
CDO.
Workspace prefixes are automatically inserted before the name of an item when adding a new item, if a 
prefix has been assigned to the highest active workspace. Workspace prefixes  indicate which workspace 
Designer is applying changes to.  For example, the prefix "C200"  would be associated to the workspace 
with a prefix set as "C200"  in Management Studio. It is recommended you use the prefix supplied by  
Designer when adding new items.
You can assign or modify workspace prefixes in Management Studio.  Refer to the Opcenter Execution 
Medical Device and Diagnostics System Administration Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core System 
Administration Guide for information.
Note: The base application workspace (csi) cannot have a workspace prefix assigned to it.
Automatically Generated Associated CDOs and References
When you add a Container CDO, if you click the check box on the Create a new CDO Definition window to 
Create & Configure a Start Service, these required tasks  are performed automatically. This table defines the 
required tasks.
Task Definition
Add a Start Service Subclass A subclass of the Start Service CDO is added to handle the Start 
service for the new Container CDO, and the container reference in 
the Start Service CDO is changed to reference the new Container 
CDO.
Add a StartDetails Subentity 
Subclass
ServiceDetails CDOs contain the change data about the Container 
transactions performed by its StartService, so every StartService 
CDO needs its own StartDetails CDO. A new StartDetails subentity 
CDO is added under the new StartService subclass.
Add a StartHistoryDetail SubclassA subclass of the StartHistoryDetail CDO is created for the new 
container CDO. This new CDO will be used to track changes to your 
Container as it is involved in transactions.

Task Definition
Change CDO References If the Service CDO uses a ServiceDetails CDO, the Service CDO 
must reference its ServiceDetails subentity through the Details 
field on the Service CDO. If a ServiceDetails subentity exists for a 
Container, then the Container field in the ServiceDetails subentity 
is consulted, so the subentity must reference the correct Container 
CDO. If you click the check box on the Create a new CDO Definition 
window to Create & Configure a Start Service, this task is

How to Remove a Container CDO Subclass
If you need to delete the Container CDO subclass that you created, you must remove the changes in the 
reverse order that you entered them. Generally, if a CDO has fields referencing its parent or child CDOs, 
you must remove those reference fields before you can remove the CDO. 
Follow these steps to remove a Container CDO subclass:
1. Clear overrides in fields containing references to parent and child CDOs for each CDO created.
For example, the new service details subentity CDO  (MyContainerStartDetails) has a Container 
field referencing the Container subclass CDO (MyContainer), and a Parent field referencing the 
subentity’s parent CDO. Clear the overrides for both of those fields. Do this in the Fields tab for 
the subentity CDO. Refer to “Creating Field Overrides” for details on overriding fields.
2. In the service CDO subclass (MyContainerStart), clear the override in the Details field 
referencing the service details subentity (MyContainerStartDetails) and in the HistorySummary 
field referencing MyContainerHistoryDetail.
3. In the service details subentity (MyContainerStartDetails), clear the overrides for the 
ChildContainers field.
4. Remove the new Service CDO subclass (MyContainerStart) by selecting it in the CDO selection 
tree, and clicking on the Remove button.
5. Remove the new Container subclass CDO (MyContainer).
6. Remove the new Service subentity CDO (MyContainerStartDetails).
7. Remove the new HistoryDetails subentity CDO (MyContainerHistoryDetail).
8. Click Update DB. A dialog box appears asking you to confirm you want to save the current MDB 
file and update the database according to the settings in the Preferences dialog box.
9. Click Yes. A status bar appears at the bottom of the window to indicate the update is in 
progress. The application saves and updates the database.

Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
Defining a NamedDataObject Subclass
A NamedDataObject CDO is a class of objects that is identified by a unique name. It represents static 
entities that do not have revisions. Named data objects have associated maintenance objects. When you 
add NDOs, follow the structure in the class library by relating your new CDOs to one another in the same 
way that the default named data objects relate to one another.
When Defining a NamedDataObject Subclass
Instances of named data objects are persistent; that is, information about them is written to the database.
Workspace prefixes are automatically inserted before the name of an item when adding a new item, if a 
prefix has been assigned to the highest active workspace. Workspace prefixes  indicate which workspace 
Designer is applying changes to.  For example, the prefix "C200"  would be associated to the workspace 
with a prefix set as "C200"  in Management Studio. It is recommended you use the prefix supplied by  
Designer when adding new items.
You can assign or modify workspace prefixes in Management Studio.  Refer to the Opcenter Execution 
Medical Device and Diagnostics System Administration Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core System 
Administration Guide for information.
Note: The base application workspace (csi) cannot have a workspace prefix assigned to it.
Overview of Tasks
The overall process to define a NamedDataObject CDO is similar to that used to define a Container CDO. 
The only additional concepts are adding an ObjectChanges CDO and a Maintenance Service CDO for the 
NDO; these steps are performed automatically at the time you create the NDO when you check Create & 
Configure a Maintenance Service. Refer to "Automated Steps in Defining a CDO Subclass" for information.
How to Add a NamedDataObject CDO Subclass
Follow these steps to add a NamedDataObject CDO subclass:
1. Open Designer.
2. Click CDOs on the toolbar. The CDO Definition window appears.
3. Select Advanced on the View menu.
4. Expand the NamedDataObject class.
5. Select a subclass of NamedDataObject. We will use Factory for this example.
6. Click the Add button under the CDO list, or right-click on Factory and select Add Subclass. The 
Create a new CDO Definition window appears.
Note: The Derive from field indicates the base class for your new CDO.
7. Enter the name MyFactory in the New CDO Name field. 
Important:The name of the CDO can be up to 30 characters in length, and cannot contain 
spaces, illegal characters, or reserved words. Refer to "Valid Characters and 
Reserved Words" for information.

Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
Defining a RevisionedObject Subclass
The RevisionedObject class in Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR is used for objects that have a name 
and a revision. The RevisionedObject class supports a revision-of-record feature that allows you to specify 
one version as the default version, allowing other objects to reference an instance of a RevisionedObject 
(RO) without specifying a revision. Examples of ROs are Document, Product, Workflow, and Spec 
(specification).
Role of the RevisionBase CDO
RevisionBase is an NDO that provides the Revision of Record and revisions. The RevOfRcd attribute 
provides the ID of the current Revision of Record. The Revisions attribute provides a list of revision 
instances associated with the revisioned object. For each RevisionBase, there is at least one RO. 
Overview of Tasks
The overall steps to define a RO are similar to those used to define a NamedDataObject CDO. The only 
additional steps address the addition of a RevisionBase CDO and changing the CDO associations for the RO 
and the RevisionBase CDOs. These are performed automatically if you select the Create & Configure a 
Maintenance Service option.
Add a RevisionedObject CDO Subclass and Add Fields
Add a RevisionedObject CDO to represent your revisioned object. If you add a CDO, it is derived from the 
previously existing CDO. This makes the one you add a CDO subclass, inheriting all the fields and 
properties of the CDO from which it is derived. After the CDO is added, you can override field properties, 
add new fields, or delete fields to create a unique CDO that fits your business model. Add fields to the 
newly added RevisionedObject (RO) as needed. Refer to “Defining List Fields” for information about adding 
list fields to a CDO.
Workspace prefixes are automatically inserted before the name of an item when adding a new item, if a 
prefix has been assigned to the highest active workspace. Workspace prefixes  indicate which workspace 
Designer is applying changes to.  For example, the prefix "C200"  would be associated to the workspace 
with a prefix set as "C200" the highest active workspace in Management Studio. It is recommended you 
use the prefix supplied by  Designer when adding new items.
You can assign or modify workspace prefixes in Management Studio.  Refer to the Opcenter Execution 
Medical Device and Diagnostics System Administration Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core System 
Administration Guide for information.
Note: The base application workspace (csi) cannot have a workspace prefix assigned to it.
Add a Subentity to an RO
There are many circumstances that are best managed by adding subentities to ROs. You may want to add 
several subentities to a single RO to group discrete types of information, with the advantage of connecting 
the grouped information to the RO. For example, to group resource information about an RO, such as a 
spec, you will maintain the resource information on a subentity under the RO Specification. 
4-52 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 115 ===

Refer to “Defining a Subentity CDO Subclass” for information on adding subentities.

Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
Defining a Subentity CDO Subclass
A subentity is an object that is owned by another object. (This ownership type of relationship is usually 
referred to as a composition.) A subentity object belongs to one and only one parent object. Any 
subentities are usually expected to live and die with the parent; if the instance of a parent is deleted, 
instances of its subentities will also be deleted.
When Defining a Subentity CDO Subclass
A subentity design is required when a list of items is needed, where each item has multiple attributes. For 
example, to model a Workflow in the Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR object model, it is required to 
define Step as a subentity, and mark step as a list field in the Workflow. 
It is also appropriate to use a subentity instead of adding multiple individual fields to the parent object 
when you would like to group a set of related fields together for reuse, encapsulation, or other purposes. 
For example, Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR includes a SchedulingDetail object that contains fields 
such as SetupTime, RunRate, and so on. This subentity definition is used in the Operation, Spec, and 
WorkflowStep objects.
While only one particular parent object owns any given subentity, the subentity can be referenced by 
other objects. For example, a workflow owns its steps, but other objects such as a container can reference 
a particular workflow step. To reference another object's subentity, it is usually necessary to specify the 
parent of the subentity as part of the reference information.
Some of the features of a subentity CDO include:
• The parent object has references to its subentities, and each subentity has a reference to its 
parent. 
• When an instance of a parent object is deleted, all instances of its subentities are automatically 
deleted.
• Either Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR includes a special class of subentity called 
NamedSubentity. Named subentities are useful when you need to create a subentity list field and 
it is appropriate to give each subentity item a name that is unique within the parent. For example, 
the VendorItem subentity used as list within a Vendor is a named subentity; the system requires 
each item to have a name that is unique within the Vendor, and the VendorItem subentity can be 
referenced by specifying the vendor and the name of the item.
• Any object can contain subentities. Even subentities can contain other subentities.
Workspace prefixes are automatically inserted before the name of an item when adding a new item, if a 
prefix has been assigned to the highest active workspace. Workspace prefixes  indicate which workspace 
Designer is applying changes to.  For example, the prefix "C200" would be associated to the workspace 
with a prefix set as "C200" the highest active workspace in Management Studio. It is recommended you 
use the prefix supplied by  Designer when adding new items.
You can assign or modify workspace prefixes in Management Studio.  Refer to the Opcenter Execution 
Medical Device and Diagnostics System Administration Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core System 
Administration Guide for information.
Note: The base application workspace (csi) cannot have a workspace prefix assigned to it.
4-56 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 119 ===
Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
Subentities of NDOs and ROs
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR has highly automated the process of creating subentities for NDOs 
and ROs. The only steps that are required are:
• Create the subentity by adding a field on the parent object. 
When adding the field, choose the [Create a new subentity] datatype. You will then be given the 
opportunity to pick what kind of subentity to create (such as a named subentity or a subclass of 
some particular existing subentity).
• Specify the attributes of the subentity, such as its name, description, storage category, and table 
name (if necessary), and specify whether or not the subentity should be used as a list in the 
current parent.
• Once you have created the subentity by adding it as a field on the parent object, navigate to the 
new subentity by right-clicking on the subentity field and using the Position to CDO shortcut 
menu option.
• Add any necessary fields to the subentity.
The Designer automatically handles all other work that enables the new subentity to be updated with the 
appropriate maintenance service.
Results of adding a new Subentity through the automated feature: 
• A new subentity is created of the type that you selected from which to inherit. 
• The new subentity contains a field that links it to the parent CDO for which you created this 
subentity. Its field name is Parent, and its type is the name of the parent CDO. 
• The parent CDO (the CDO for which you created the new subentity) has a new field containing a 
reference to its new subentity. The field type is the name of the new subentity.
Subentities for Containers
The Container CDO has a subentity, ContainerDetails, referenced by the Detail field. In certain cases, you 
might need to add fields to customize the Container CDO. You can add them to the ContainerDetails 
subentity. ContainerDetails is a non-list subentity.  Refer to “Subentities of Other Objects” for subentity lists.
Subentities of Other Objects
If a subentity of another subentity, a subentity of a Shop Floor service, a subentity list for a container, or 
other sophisticated subentities are necessary, more Designer configuration will be needed. Necessary 
steps depend on the particular task, but might include:
• Generating the new subentity, either at the time the field is added to the parent, or beforehand 
by creating a new subclass of a subentity object.
• Creating parallel history subentities, or extending an existing history object to include the fields 
present in the service or container subentity.
• Extending or updating existing maps to copy the data as necessary, for example between a 
service subentity and a history subentity.
• Updating CLFs to add new maps or other mechanisms of copying data.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 4-57

=== PDF PAGE 120 ===
Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
• If appropriate, update the Parent field of the new subentity to be of a particular type of object.
Adding an Existing Subentity to a Parent CDO
An existing subentity can be easily added to an object by selecting its object type. On the Add a new CDO 
field window, select the data type Object and subsequently select the name of the existing subentity in the 
Field type selection list. You can then mark this field as a list and add corresponding field definitions to 
service related objects as required.
Additional steps may be required depending on the type of object to which you added this subentity field, 
like extending maps, and so forth. These steps were described previously.
Exercise to Add a Subentity Through a Field
In this example, you will add a subentity subclass that groups together data about contacts that you want 
to maintain about each of your customers. This subentity allows you to define and collect information in a 
subentity for each instance of Customer, and bidirectionally reference each of the Customers to its 
Contacts subentity. 
Important:Adding subentities is automated through the Add option in the Fields tab for CDOs. This 
means that your first step is to identify and select the CDO you intend to extend with a 
new subentity.
Referencing a Named Subentity by Name
Named subentities can be referenced either by name or by index. When you have a named subentity on an 
object (such as a product), unique entries can be added by Name with a corresponding value. 
Therefore, Designer allows you to reference the subentity either by index or the specific entry in a path 
selector by name instead of index.
For example, to search for the following by name:
Container.Product.Attributes("Color").Value. 
You can use the following:
FindListItem (Container.Product.Attributes, Name, “Color”).Value
