Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
Defining a Service CDO Subclass
Service objects represent the work performed on Container objects. In Defining a Container CDO Subclass, 
the Start service subclass is automatically created. In Defining a NamedDataObject Subclass and Defining a 
RevisionedObject Subclass, a Maintenance service subclass is automatically created. Other services are 
available, such as inquiries and transactions. Most day-to-day tasks performed by employees are through 
shop floor transactions on containers. 
When Defining a Service CDO Subclass
The process of defining a Service CDO subclass is illustrated in this diagram. These steps are further 
described in the sections that follow.
Workspace prefixes are automatically inserted before the name of an item when adding a new item, if a 
prefix has been assigned to the highest active workspace. Workspace prefixes  indicate which workspace 
Designer is applying changes to.  For example, the prefix "C200"  would be associated to the workspace 
with a prefix set as "C200"  in Management Studio. It is recommended you use the prefix supplied by  
Designer when adding new items.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 4-63

=== PDF PAGE 126 ===
Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
You can assign or modify workspace prefixes in Management Studio.  Refer to the Opcenter Execution 
Medical Device and Diagnostics System Administration Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core System 
Administration Guide for information.
Note: The base application workspace (csi) cannot have a workspace prefix assigned to it.
Task Overview
The Service class contains subclasses representing the Container transactions performed on the shop floor, 
such as the Start, MoveTxn, and Associate CDOs, to name a few. A step-by-step exercise follows this 
general discussion.
Add a Service CDO subclass
You will add your service subclass under the appropriate Siemens-provided service CDO. 
Defining a service object, for example the MoveStd transaction under ContainerTxn, uses the same 
procedure as used for CDOs in general. No database tables are associated with transactions, although 
history information is collected and stored to keep track of the transactions used on a Container, when the 
transactions were performed, and who performed them.
Create a ServiceDetails subentity
Some services have a ServiceDetails subentity containing information needed by the service. The simple 
services do not need a ServiceDetails subentity; all the information needed by the service are in the service 
itself. For services that have a ServiceDetails subentity, you will create a ServiceDetails subentity subclass 
for the new service CDO subclass. Concepts and instructions were explained in Defining a Subentity CDO 
Subclass and demonstrated in Exercise to Add a Subentity Through a Field.
Add fields to the service or ServiceDetails CDO subclass
For service CDOs with a ServiceDetails field, you will add the fields to the ServiceDetails subentity. For 
service CDOs without a ServiceDetails field (or its equivalent, like ToContainerDetails for the Split service), 
you will add the fields directly to the service CDO.
Override CDO references
If the new service CDO inherits a Details field, the field points to its parent CDO’s Service Details subentity, 
rather than to the ServiceDetails subentity belonging to the newly created service CDO. In this case, you 
will change the reference in the Details field of the new service CDO to point to the new ServiceDetails 
subentity CDO. 
The new service CDO needs to point to the correct Container CDO. For services that have a ServiceDetails 
subentity, you will override the Container field in the subentity to point to the correct Container CDO. For 
services that do not have a ServiceDetails subentity, you will override the Container field in the service 
CDO to point to the correct Container CDO. 
Add fields to the History CDO
If you extend a service by creating a subclass and adding your own fields, and if you want those new fields 
to be written to the service’s history records, you will add the same fields to the appropriate type of History 
CDO subclass and then map these additional fields from the extended service CDO to the new History CDO 
subclass. The three types of History CDOs for your use include:
• ServiceHistoryDetail (for services that have ServiceDetails).
4-64 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 127 ===
Chapter4:ConfigurableDataObjects
• ServiceHistorySummary (for services that do not have ServiceDetails).
• StartHistoryDetail (for Start service only, in addition to ServiceHistoryDetail).
Add fields to the HistoryDetails subentity CDO
Any field that you intend to record must be copied to the HistoryDetails CDO from the service CDO. Proper 
recording of data will occur if you map the fields from the source CDO to its destination CDO. 
First you will add a subclass to the appropriate HistoryDetails subentity, and then add the new fields to this 
History CDO. This will enable you to record historical information for the new Container fields every time 
the service is performed against the Container. 
How to Add a Service CDO
The Start service and all the associated CDOs and references are automatically created when you create a 
subclass of the Container CDO and select Create & Configure a Start Service. Maintenance services are 
automatically created when you add a subclass of a NamedDataObject or a Revisioned Object CDO and 
select Create & Configure a Maintenance Service. For all other container services, you need to create a 
subclass for the needed service.

This window enables you to add a new subclass to this  Service CDO. 
4. Enter the name of this new service subclass, MyThruput in the New CDO Name field. 
Important:The name of the CDO can be as many as 30 characters in length, and cannot 
contain spaces, illegal characters, or reserved words. Refer to “Valid Characters 
and Reserved Words” for information.
5. Enter a description in the Description field. The Create button is enabled if both Name and 
Description fields are filled.
6. Keep the default values for CDO Usage of Service Shopfloor Reversible and Storage Category 
of None.
Service CDOs are non-persistent and do not require storage.
7. Click Create. MyThruput is now a subclass of Thruput.
8. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.

Chapter 5: Automated Steps in 
Defining a CDO Subclass
Introduction
Defining a CDO involves more than simply selecting a CDO in the class library and clicking Add. The 
process of adding a CDO requires fitting the new CDO into the Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX structure. 
This involves knowing when to create subentities and fields, when to map, when to override, and so on. 
Most of this process is automated in Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR. 
This chapter explains the concepts behind the automated procedures. It contains flowcharts and 
descriptions of the procedures to define Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR objects, as well as exercises 
describing the process.
In This Chapter
This chapter contains these topics:
• Defining a Container CDO Subclass
• Defining a NamedDataObject Subclass
• Defining a RevisionedObject Subclass
• Defining a ChgContainerAttribute Subclass
• Creating the Service Subclass Using the Wizard
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 5-1

=== PDF PAGE 132 ===
Chapter5:AutomatedStepsinDefiningaCDOSubclass
Defining a Container CDO Subclass
Container CDO subclasses are one type of CDO, and the process of defining a Container CDO subclass is 
depicted in this image. 
The following tasks are performed automatically if you click the check box on the Create a new CDO 
Definition window to Create & Configure a Start Service when you add a Container CDO.
• Add a StartService CDO Subclass
• Add a StartDetails Subentity Subclass
• Add a StartHistoryDetail Subclass
• Adding Container Fields to the Service History Details CDO
If the Service CDO uses a ServiceDetails CDO, the Service CDO must reference its ServiceDetails subentity 
through the Details field on the Service CDO. If a ServiceDetails subentity exists for a Container, then the 
Container field in the ServiceDetails subentity is consulted, so the subentity must reference the correct 
Container CDO. If you click the check box on the Create a new CDO Definition window to Create & 
Configure a Start Service, this task is performed automatically for you. Refer to "Creating Field Overrides" 
for information on overriding CDO references.
5-2 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 133 ===
Chapter5:AutomatedStepsinDefiningaCDOSubclass
When you add fields to a Container CDO, if you click the check box on the Add Fields to the Associated 
Service Objects, the required tasks listed below are performed automatically:
• Add fields to the StartDetails CDO subclass
• Add fields to the StartHistoryDetails CDO subclass
• Map fields between CDOs
Mapping fields updates fields in your specified CDO destinations from the CDO sources you 
identify. Fields added to the new Container subclass, to the StartServiceDetails, and to the 
StartHistoryDetails CDOs are mapped from the new fields in the StartDetails CDO to the new Con-
tainer fields, and then mapped from the new Container CDO to the new StartHistoryDetails CDO. 
Mapping is done in this sequence because the end-user’s changes will go from the User Interface 
to the Start Details CDO first. Refer to "Viewing Field Maps" for information.
Add a StartService CDO Subclass
Every Container CDO has to have its own StartService CDO. The procedure to create a StartService CDO for 
the new Container has been automated for you if you click Create & Configure a Start Service when 
creating the new Container CDO. A subclass of the Start Service CDO is added to handle the start service 
for the new Container CDO, and the container reference in the Start Service CDO is changed to reference 
the new Container CDO.
Refer to  "How to Add a Service CDO" for information.
Add a StartDetails Subentity Subclass
ServiceDetails CDOs contain the change data (details) about the Container transactions performed by its 
StartService, so every StartService CDO needs its own StartDetails CDO. A new StartDetails subentity CDO 
is added under the new StartService subclass. This procedure is automated for you if you click Create & 
Configure a Start Service when creating a Container CDO.
Refer to "Creating the Service Subclass Using the Wizard" for information.
Add a StartHistoryDetail Subclass
A subclass of the StartHistoryDetail CDO is created for the new container CDO. This new CDO will be used 
to track changes to your Container as it is involved in transactions. This CDO is automatically created for 
you if you click Create & Configure a Start Service when creating a Container CDO.
Adding Container Fields to the Service History Details CDO
In this exercise, you are adding fields to the History Details CDO to capture history data from MyField1 and 
MyField2 that you added to MyContainer for historical recording purposes. If you click Add Fields to 
Associated Service Objects when adding the fields, this step is automated for you.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 5-3

Chapter5:AutomatedStepsinDefiningaCDOSubclass

Follow these steps to add fields:
1. Open Designer.
2. Locate the following CDO in the  CDO class library:
Subentity>HistoryDetails>StartHistoryDetail>MyContainerHistory Detail
3. With MyContainerHistoryDetail selected, select the Fields tab.
4. Add the same fields MyField1 and MyField2 to MyContainerHistoryDetail, in the same way you

fields are updated when the Container CDO is updated. To connect them properly, you 
must map the fields. Mapping is explained in "Viewing Field Maps." If you click Add 
Fields to Associated Service Objects when adding the fields, this step is automated for 
you.
5. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.

=== PDF PAGE 135 ===
Chapter5:AutomatedStepsinDefiningaCDOSubclass
Defining a NamedDataObject Subclass
A NamedDataObject is a class of  objects that is identified by a unique name. It represents static entities 
that are not revisioned. When you add named data objects, follow the structure in the class library by 
relating your new CDOs to one another in the same way that the default named data objects relate to one 
another. Instances of named data objects are persistent; that is, information about them is written to the 
database.
This image shows the process to create a NamedDataObject CDO. Most of the steps are automatically 
created if you select Create & Configure a Maintenance Service when you add an NDO subclass.
When you add a NamedDataObject CDO, if you select the check box on the Create a new CDO Definition 
window to Create a Maintenance Service, the required tasks (listed immediately below) are performed 
automatically:
• Add an ObjectChanges CDO for the NDO.
• Reference the new NDO from new ObjectChanges CDO.
• Add a Maintenance CDO for the NDO.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 5-5

=== PDF PAGE 136 ===
Chapter5:AutomatedStepsinDefiningaCDOSubclass
• Reference the new NDO from the new Maintenance CDO
ObjectChanges CDO for the NDO
ObjectChanges CDOs are visible in the Advanced view. The purpose of adding an ObjectChanges CDO is to 
provide a scratch area to store the changes before committing them to the NamedDataObject CDO. The 
ObjectChanges CDO is exposed to the Client. This means that if changes are made to the ObjectChanges 
CDO, the changes are validated before they are written to the NamedDataObject CDO. This feature 
provides a layer of protection from accidental changes. The validated changes to the NamedDataObject 
CDO are in turn written to the database.
Reference the new NDO from new ObjectChanges CDO
The reference is made with a field override. Linking these objects together through a field changes its field 
type on the ObjectChanges CDO to the name of the NDO to which it is being linked. Moving CDO object 
references into fields enables CDOs to reference each other. The key field that contains this link on the 
ObjectChanges CDO is called ObjectToChange. Refer to "Creating Field Overrides" for information on the 
process of overriding.
Maintenance CDO for the NDO 
The purpose of adding a Maintenance CDO is to maintain the new NamedDataObject CDO. Maintenance 
includes loading, adding, copying, changing, and deleting instances of the object from a Client 
application, such as the Portal.
Reference the new NDO from the new Maintenance CDO
With this step, you link the Maintenance CDO to the new NamedDataObject CDO and to the new 
ObjectChanges CDO.
5-6 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 137 ===
Chapter5:AutomatedStepsinDefiningaCDOSubclass
Defining a RevisionedObject Subclass
There are specific steps that are required when creating a RevisionedObject CDO, as shown in this image. 
When creating the RO, you can select the option to automatically create the required associated CDOs and 
required fields. 
The overall steps to define a RevisionedObject are similar to those used to define a NamedDataObject 
CDO. The only additional steps address adding a RevisionBase CDO and changing the CDO associations for 
the RevisionedObject and the RevisionBase CDOs, which are performed automatically if you select the 
check box on the Create a new CDO Definition window to Create a Maintenance Service.
The RevisionBase CDO
RevisionBase is a named data object for providing the ROR (revision of record) and revisions. The ROR 
attribute provides the ID of the current revision of record. The Revisions attribute provides a list of revision 
instances associated with the revisioned object. For each RevisionBase, there is at least one 
RevisionedObject. 
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 5-7

=== PDF PAGE 138 ===
Chapter5:AutomatedStepsinDefiningaCDOSubclass
Automatically Generated Associated CDOs
When you add a RevisionedObject (RO), if you select the check box to Create & Configure a Maintenance 
Service, the actions listed immediately below are performed automatically:
• Add a RevisionedObjectChanges CDO
The purpose of adding an ObjectChanges CDO is to provide a scratch area to hold changes before 
committing them to the RevisionedObject. The ObjectChanges CDO is exposed to the Client. This 
means that if changes are made to the ObjectChanges CDO, the changes are validated first before 
they are written to the RevisionedObject. This feature provides a layer of protection from acci-
dental changes. The validated changes to the RevisionedObject are in turn written to the data-
base.
• Add a RevisionBase CDO (actually a NamedDataObject CDO)
The purpose of adding a RevisionBase CDO is to contain the common things associated with all 
the revisions for this object, such as the name, the list of IDs, and the revision of record.
• Link the RevisionedObject (RO) and the ObjectChanges CDO
Overriding field properties within a CDO is a method of linking two CDOs together. Inherited 
fields inherit the field properties from the superclass from which the CDO is derived. The process 
of overriding a field is changing a field property of an inherited field for the specified CDO. Link-
ing objects together through a field is the process of changing the field type to be the name of 
the CDO to which it is being linked. This causes the field within one CDO to point to another CDO. 
Here you are linking the ObjectChanges CDO to the RevisionedObject CDO.
• Add a maintenance CDO to maintain the RevisionedObject
The purpose of adding a Maintenance CDO is to maintain the RevisionedObject CDO.
• Reference the new RevisionedObject in the Maintenance CDO
The purpose is the same as for creating an override in the ObjectChanges CDO except here you 
are linking the Maintenance CDO to the RevisionedObject, ObjectChanges, and the RevisionBase 
CDOs.
• Change the CDO associations for RevisionedObject and RevisionBase CDO
The purpose of changing the CDO associations is to link the RevisionedObject CDO to the Revi-
sionBase CDO.
5-8 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

When Creating the Service Subclass Using the Wizard
Workspace prefixes are automatically inserted before the name of an item when adding a new item, if a 
prefix has been assigned to the highest active workspace. Workspace prefixes  indicate which workspace 
Designer is applying changes to.  For example, the prefix "C200" would be associated to the workspace 
with a prefix set as "C200"  in Management Studio. Siemens recommends you use the prefix supplied by  
Designer when adding new items.
You can assign or modify workspace  prefixes in Management Studio.  Refer to the Opcenter Execution 
Medical Device and Diagnostics System Administration Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core System 
Administration Guide for information.
Note: The base application workspace (csi) cannot have a workspace prefix assigned to it.
Change Attributes Services Wizard
Changing the service involves adding, removing, and renaming fields. Instead of making the changes to 
the CDOs directly, Siemens recommends making the changes through the Change Attribute Services 
Wizard to ensure that the changes are propagated through the associated history objects and that 
mapping information is also updated.
The ChgContainerAttribute Service Wizard: 
• Is enabled for multi-level sub classes of ChgContainerAttribute service 
• Disabled updates to inherited field maps, displayed in light green color 
• Includes a Sub-class wizard to display subtypes of the Container type defined in the parent service 
instead of displaying all the Container subtypes
Note: The Change Attribute Services wizard is available through the CDO shortcut menu for 
any ChgContainerAttribute subclass. In our example, the wizard is called out through 
the MyContainerMaint subclass.

1. Log in to Designer.
2. Click CDOs on the toolbar.
3. Locate and select the ChgContainerAttribute CDO.

service.

• MyContainerMaintDetailToContainerDetail
For the next two maps, if you added fields to CurrentStatus and ContainerDetail, these are not 
mapped automatically. You need to copy the user fields to MyContainerDetail and then map 
those fields.
Important:After the service is created, do not change the container type because redoing 
the maps is a manual and complex process.

Chapter 6: Configurable Logic Flows
Introduction
A Configurable Logic Flow (CLF) is a sequence of functions performing one or more tasks. Logic flows are 
the way that user-configurable business processes are implemented.  Most occur at the server level where 
the user defines the business rules and logic. (High Performance CLFs are processed on the database 
rather than the application server.) CLFs are used to uniquely define how Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter 
EX CR responds to a CDO event. This chapter covers the concepts related to CLFs within Opcenter EX MDD 
and Opcenter EX CR.
In This Chapter
This chapter contains these topics:
• Introduction to CLFs
• CLFs Window
• Viewing CLFs
• Adding User-Defined CLFs to the CLF Library
• Removing CLFs
• Adding Functions to CLFs and Viewing a CLF’s Functions
• Reordering Functions
• Disabling or Removing Functions
• Modifying Default Function Parameter Values for a CLF
• Adding CLF Parameters
• Adding High Performance CLFs
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 6-1

=== PDF PAGE 146 ===
Chapter6:ConfigurableLogicFlows
Introduction to CLFs
CLFs are grouped into these categories:
1. SystemFunctions - Single function CLFs
2. DataMethod - Methods called on data objects
3. ServiceValidations - Called by the validation events of a service
4. ServiceProcessing - Called by the processing events of a service
5. OnGetValue - Called by the OnGetValue event of a field
6. SelectionValues - Called by the SelectionValues event of a field
7. OtherFieldEvents - Called by the field Initialize, Update, and Validate events
8. TemplateProcessing - Called as part of the shell processing
9. High Performance - Called by the database in the form of one or more database statements 
(INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE).
Note: High Performance Engine is a separately licensed feature. The HighPerformance CLF 
category does not appear if you are not licensed for this feature. Refer to "Adding 
High Performance CLFs" for more information.
10. UserFunctions - Written by users for users
11. Obsolete - No longer used or needed
A function has zero or more parameters and is implemented as an ActiveX (or OCX) control. Parameters 
are explained further in the Functions chapter. The function, contained within a CLF, performs the work. A 
function has access to the system’s core objects and methods that it uses to accomplish its work. Opcenter 
EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR functions have been written to perform a wide variety of tasks that include: 
simple arithmetic, searching complex data structures, database queries, report writing, data collection, 
data validation, and more. 
Note: You can modify a CLF by adding, removing, or reordering the functions contained within.
CLF Composed of Functions
You can expand the capabilities of a CLF by introducing a new function or by changing the order of the 
existing functions. While in the CLF Definitions window, you can view all of the functions listed for a CLF in 
the Logic flow selection list. The functions are displayed in the order they are executed. The Available 
Functions list displays all of the functions that can be obtained for a CLF. From this window, you can view, 
add, or remove functions from a CLF.
6-2 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

Functions Tab
The Functions tab displays details about that CLF when you expand a category and select a CLF. When you 
first click CLFs on the toolbar, the CLF definition window opens at the Functions tab. 
This image shows an example of the Functions tab.
The Functions tab has three areas:
• Logic Flow
This area lists the sequence of functions and their descriptions that comprise the CLF.
• Available Functions
This area lists the functions that are available to add to the logic flow. You can access functions by 
selecting them from the groups listed or by searching for them using the filter field.  Enter the first 
few characters or the entire function name in the filter field to search for a specific function. 
Entering a string displays an alphabetical list of functions with names beginning with that string. 
Select the function you want from the list or enter the entire function name and click Go.
Note: You can use database functions on standard CLFs and standard functions on High 
Performance CLFs. Refer to "Adding High Performance CLFs" for information on 
High Performance  CLFs.
• Function Parameters
This area lists information about parameters specific to the function that is selected on the logic 
flow pane. Depending on the function you selected, the following information is displayed:
• Parameter Name
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 6-5

=== PDF PAGE 150 ===
Chapter6:ConfigurableLogicFlows
• Parameter Name indicates SQLStatementName when  you are working with some 
database functions. Refer to "Adding High Performance CLFs."
• Value
• Direction
• In indicates that the parameter passes a value or reference to a CLF.
• Out indicates that the parameter is set from within the CLF.
• In/Out indicates that the parameter passes a value to the CLF and the CLF sets the 
parameter to a new value or reference.
• Return indicates that the CLF yields a value.
• Dynamic
Select the Dynamic check box if you want the parameter to be passed as a dynamic value 
using look-up expressions. Leave it unchecked if you want the parameter to be passed as an 
actual value. If the function enforces parameters type (set by the Enforce Parameter Types 
flag in the Functions window), the Dynamic check box disappears and the Multiplicity and 
Data Type columns appear.
• Multiplicity
This attribute specifies the number of values the parameter can or must take. The possible 
values are 0...* (zero or more, therefore an optional list), 0...1 (zero or one, therefore 
optional), 1...1 (one, therefore required), 1...* (one or more, therefore a required list). 
• Data Type
The Data Type column is a concatenation of the Parameter data type or type class and the 
Type Multiplicity.
Below the Logic flow area are four buttons whose use are indicated by their names:
• Append
• Insert
• Remove
• Disable
Only the Disable button is enabled by default. Append and Insert are enabled for user-defined functions 
when you select a function from available functions. Remove is enabled for user-defined functions when a 
function has been appended to or inserted in the Logic Flow.
This tab is used to view or modify the functions (and corresponding parameters) that comprise a CLF.

There are two ways you can view CLFs:

How to View the Complete List of CLFs in the Library
Follow these steps to view the complete list of CLFs in the library:
1. Open Designer.
2. Click CLFs. The CLF Definitions window appears, displaying the CLF library at the Functions tab.
3. Expand the categories and examine the CLFs under them. 
4. Select a CLF and examine the functions in its logic flow.
5. Select a function from the flow and examine the parameter definitions for the function. Repeat 
for other functions in the logic flow.
6. Examine the CLF Parameters tab if there are parameters defined for the scope of the entire CLF.
How to View CLFs Attached to a CDO or Field Events
Follow these steps to viewCLFs attached to a CDO or Field Events:
1. Open Designer.

4. Click the Where Used tab.
5. Double-click a CDO name on one of the tables. The CDO’s definition window appears.
6. If the CLF is being used by the CDO’s event:
Click the Events tab and expand the method specified in the table to view the attached CLF.
Or
If the CLF is being used by a CDO field’s event:
a. Double-click the field specified in the table to open its properties window.
b. Click the field properties’ Events tab.
c. Expand the event specified in the table. The attached CLF is displayed as a node below the 
event.

Chapter6:ConfigurableLogicFlows
Adding User-Defined CLFs to the CLF Library
You can add a user-defined CLF by creating one from scratch or by copying a default CLF and then 
extending it.
When Adding User-Defined CLFs to the CLF Library
Workspace prefixes are automatically inserted before the name of an item when adding a new item, if a 
prefix has been assigned to the highest active workspace. Workspace prefixes  indicate which workspace 
Designer is applying changes to.  For example, the prefix "C200"  would be associated to the workspace 
with a prefix set as "C200"  in Management Studio. It is recommended you use the prefix supplied by  
Designer when adding new items.
You can assign or modify workspace prefixes in Management Studio.  Refer to the Opcenter Execution 
Medical Device and Diagnostics System Administration Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core System 
Administration Guide for information.
Note: The base application workspace (csi) cannot have a workspace prefix assigned to it.
Note: For organization purposes, add a CLF or extend a default CLF in the UserFunctions category. 
However, you will not encounter problems if you add your CLFs in other categories.
How to Create a CLF
Follow these steps to create a CLF:
1. Open Designer.
2. Click CLFs.
3. Select the UserFunctions category (or select another category).
4. Click the Add button located below the CLF list box. A new CLF is added to the bottom of the 
category’s CLF list with a default name assigned to it.
Note: You can also right-click in the selection tree area and select Add CLF Definition.
When a CLF is first added, it is in the Rename mode. 
5. Enter a name for this new CLF.
6. Enter a description for this CLF in the General tab. 
7. If desired, change the setting for Visibility in Designer.
8. Add functions in the Functions tab, as desired. 
9. Click away from the CLF to save it. The CLF is added to the library, ready for use. 
Note: Later, you can attach this CLF to a CDO or field event.
10. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 6-13

=== PDF PAGE 158 ===
Chapter6:ConfigurableLogicFlows
How to Extend a Copy of a Default CLF
Follow these steps to extend a copy of a default CLF:
1. Select a default CLF, one with functionality that is similar to what you want.
2. Right-click the desired CLF and select Copy from the  list.
3. Right-click the UserFunctions category (or any category to which you want to add the copy) and 
select Paste [Name of default CLF] from the  list. A copy of the CLF is added.
It is in rename mode. The copied CLF includes the same functions and parameters as the original from 
Siemens.
4. Enter a name for this extended CLF.
5. Click the General tab and Enter a description for the CLF.
6. Customize the CLF by:
• Adding, removing, disabling, and reordering the functions on the logic flow
• Changing parameters associated with the functions (described later in this chapter)
7. Click away from the CLF to save it. The CLF is added to the library, ready for use. 
Note: Later, you can attach this CLF to a CDO or field event.
8. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.
6-14 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 159 ===
Chapter6:ConfigurableLogicFlows
Removing CLFs
You can only remove user-defined CLFs. Removing a CLF in the CLFs window removes the CLF from the 
CLF library and it will no longer show up among the choices when you want to attach a CLF to a CDO 
event or method.
If the CLF is attached to any CDO event or method, go to that CDO and detach the CLF first from those 
events or methods. If the CLF is attached to multiple CDOs, make sure to detach the CLF from all of them. 
Refer to "How to Detach a CLF From an Event or Method" for information.
How to Remove a CLF
Follow these steps to remove a CLF:
1. Open Designer.
2. Click CLFs.
3. Expand the CLF category and select the user-defined CLF to be removed.
4. Verify that the Remove button is enabled. The Remove button is disabled if the user CLF is 
attached to any CDO.
5. If the Remove button is disabled, click the WhereUsed tab to see if the user CLF is attached to any 
CDO. 
6. Repeat the procedure to select the user CLF again.
7. Click the Remove button. The CLF is removed.
8. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.

You can add functions to user CLFs. Adding functions also means you need to determine the function’s

You can select multiple functions at a time.

• To select a function from a group, expand the group that contains the function you want and 
select it. 
• To search for a function, enter the first few characters or the entire function name. Entering a 
string displays an alphabetical list of functions with names beginning with that string. Select the 
function you want from the list or enter the entire function name and click Go.

Chapter6:ConfigurableLogicFlows
Disabling or Removing Functions
You can remove functions from logic flow of user CLFs. In some cases, even if you have no use for a 
function in a CLF, you might want to preserve the function and its parameters without deleting. Disabling 
a function is therefore preferred, because you have the option to enable it again as needed. Removing a 
function from a CLF does not permanently remove the function from the library.
For example, you can disable the Call function to FromContainer.ValidateExpirationDate by disabling the 
function in the ActualsContainer_Validate CLF and the ActualsSerialContainer_Validate CLF. Refer to “How 
to Disable a Function in a User CLF” for information.
When Disabling or Removing Functions
You can disable or remove functions by:
• Selecting the function or functions in the Logic flow pane and then clicking the Disable or 
Remove buttons as needed.
• Selecting the function or functions in the Logic flow pane, right-clicking, and then selecting the 
Disable or Remove options as needed.
How to Remove a Function from a User CLF
Follow these steps to remove a function from a user CLF:
1. Open Designer.
2. Click CLFs. 
3. Select the user CLF.
4. Select the Functions tab.
5. Select the function or functions in the Logic flow pane to be removed.
6. Click Remove. A dialog box appears asking you to confirm removing the function.
7. Click Yes. The function is removed from the CLF, but not the Functions library.
8. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.
How to Disable a Function in a User CLF
Follow these steps to disable a function in a user CLF:
1. Open Designer.
2. Click CLFs. 
3. Select the user CLF.
4. Select the Functions tab.
5. Select the function or functions in the Logic flow pane to be disabled.

=== PDF PAGE 165 ===
Chapter6:ConfigurableLogicFlows
Modifying Default Function Parameter Values for a CLF
When you add a new CLF to a CDO, many of the parameter values for the functions are already provided. 
These are referred to as the CLF default parameter values. To modify these default values, the definition of 
the CLF must be modified. Note that when the default parameter values are changed, only the template 
version of the CLF will be affected. Consequently, parameter values of a CLF and its functions already 
attached to an event or method will not be affected. If you want to use the modified parameter values for 
the CLF previously attached to an event or method, detach the CLF there and attach the modified CLF 
definition.
These modifications will affect only the function for the selected CLF. Refer to “Editing Parameter Values 
for a Function” for information on modifying the function parameters in general.
How to Modify Default Parameters in a Function for a CLF
Follow these steps to modify default parameters in a function for a CLF:
1. Open Designer.
2. Click CLFs. 
3. Select the CLF from the selection window.
The example shows MoveIn_Execute under ServiceProcessing.
4. Select the function to be modified in the Logic flow pane. The default parameter values for the 
selected function appear in the Function parameters block. 
5. Modify default parameter values as desired.
6. Optionally, select the Dynamic check box to have the parameter  passed as a dynamic value using 
look-up expressions. Leave it deselected if you want the parameter to be passed as an actual 
value.
Note: If the function enforces parameter types (set by a flag in the Functions window), the 
Dynamic check box will not be visible and Multiplicity and Data Type columns will be 
visible instead. Multiplicity and Data Type are read-only in this window. To change the 
values for these columns, you must go to the Function definition window where you 
can modify user-defined functions.
7. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 6-21

Chapter6:ConfigurableLogicFlows
Adding CLF Parameters
You can add CLF parameters to user-defined CLFs.
When Adding CLF Parameters
While the values for function parameters may be set by the user at configuration time, values for CLF 
parameters are set by some functions such as Call and If as they run. CLF parameters are available for the 
life of the CLF and are prefixed with CLF::. High Performance CLF parameters are prefixed with DBCLF::.
If you want to use the added parameters for the CLF that had been previously attached to an event or 
method, detach the CLF there and attach the modified CLF definition.
How to Add CLF Parameters
Follow these steps to add CLF parameters:
1. Open Designer.
2. Click CLFs. The CLF Definitions window appears.
3. Select the user CLF to which you want to add parameters.
4. Click the CLF Parameters tab.
5. Click Add. A generic parameter appears on the parameter list pane with the default name of 
Parameter1.
6. Enter a new name in the Parameter name field.
7. Click the generic parameter to save the name. The new name replaces the generic name.
8. Select an entry for Special processing.
9. Select a Direction for the CLF parameter.
10. Select an entry for Multiplicity.
11. Enter a Description of your CLF parameter.
12. Select a data type and type multiplicity from the Data Type and Type Multiplicity lists.
13. Enter notes and comments according to your business requirements.
14. Click away from the Parameters tab to save your changes.
15. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.
6-22 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 167 ===
Chapter6:ConfigurableLogicFlows
Adding High Performance CLFs
High Performance CLFs  are a category of CLFs that are processed on the database rather than the 
application server. A High Performance CLF is defined as part of a transaction and is sent to the database in 
the form of one or more database statements (INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE). Executing SQL statements on 
the database makes processing more efficient.
High Performance Engine is a separately licensed feature. The High Performance CLF category does not 
appear if you are not licensed for this feature.
Refer to the Opcenter Execution Core Technical Reference: High Performance Engine or the Opcenter 
Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Technical Reference: High Performance Engine for information 
on High Performance Engine.
When Adding High Performance CLFs
The application displays standard functions and database-specific functions in the list of Available 
Functions. You can add, rename, and remove High Performance CLFs like other CLFs.
When you add a  function to a High Performance CLF, parameters are available for the life of the High 
Performance CLF and are prefixed with DBCLF::. DBCLF:: variables are created by the database stored 
procedures. DBCLF:: variable references  are used only on the database. The application server ignores the 
DBCLF:: prefix and passes it  to the database.
When you select some  database functions such as ExecuteSingleSQL in the Available Functions list and 
drag it to the Logic Flow area, the application populates the Parameter Name in the Function Parameters 
area  with SQLStatementName. Other database functions do not have the SQLStatementName parameter; 
for example, SQLParameter has the Value parameter with a data type of Any and Multiplicity set to 0..1 
SQLStatementName uses Special Processing. The Select a Query pop-up appears when you click the ellipsis 
for this field. You can select an existing query, edit an existing query, or create a new query using this pop-
up. Only  Advanced Query and NonQuery SQL types of queries are available. The Include all Workspaces 
check box is not selected by default, indicating that only queries within your workspace are available for 
selection. Selecting this check box enables you to select a query from other workspaces.
The Query Definitions pop-up appears if you select to edit a query.  You can edit only queries defined in the 
same workspace in which you are working.
Any parameters associated with the query are returned to the High Performance CLF after you select or 
edit a query. The application populates the Value column for the SQLStatementName parameter with the 
query name.   The list of all query parameters displays in the Logic Flow area.  This enables you to  populate 
the parameters associated with the query.
If you try to change the query to a new query, a warning appears asking if you want to reset the SQL 
parameter list for the current SQL statement. If yes, the application resets the query name and the 
parameters.  If not, the application preserves the existing parameters .
You can click the New button in the Select a Query  pop-up to create a new query.  An empty Query 
Definitions pop-up appears.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 6-23

Chapter7:Fields
Field Type Definitions
Field type definitions function as a data dictionary, providing a finite set of data elements that are used in 
the Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR. All CDO fields are based on the set of data elements defined by 
this data dictionary. Field types are grouped under data types.
This topic discusses the following:
• Data Type and Field Type
• Field Type Properties
• Adding or Modifying Field Types
Workspace prefixes are automatically inserted before the name of an item when adding a new item, if a 
prefix has been assigned to the highest active workspace. Workspace prefixes  indicate which workspace 
Designer is applying changes to.  For example, the prefix "C200"  would be associated to the workspace 
with a prefix set as "C200"  in Management Studio. It is recommended you use the prefix supplied by  
Designer when adding new items.
You can assign or modify workspace prefixes in Management Studio.  Refer to the Opcenter Execution 
Medical Device and Diagnostics System Administration Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core System 
Administration Guide for information.
Note: The base application workspace (csi) cannot have a workspace prefix assigned to it.
Data Type and Field Type
Data Type is the element by which field types are categorized. Each data type yields a list of available field 
types that are used to define unique fields. 
This table provides the data type, definition, and the field type for each data type definition. 
Data Type Definition Field Types
Boolean True or False [Boolean], IsEnabled, 
IsFrozen, 
IsRevisionOfRecord
7-6 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 179 ===
Chapter7:Fields
Data Type Definition Field Types
Decimal Fixed point type, an exact numeric value as opposed to 
an approximate numeric type like float or real. It is 
defined by a precision and scale. The precision p refers to 
the overall number of digits both to the left and right of 
the decimal point. The scale s refers to the number of 
digits to the right of the decimal point, also referred to as 
fractional component. 
When working with the Decimal data type, consider the 
following guidelines:
• The application server supports a maximum 
precision of 29, which is the static default 
setting, and a dynamic scale. Exceeding 
precision will result in an exception when saving 
the definition.
• The Designer-specified values for precision and 
scale are ignored by the application server.
• The Designer-specified values for precision and 
scale are enforced by the database management 
system (DBMS). Exceeding precision or scale 
handling is dependent on the DBMS that you are 
using. Consult your database administrator or 
database reference manual for details on 
decimal types, limitations, and error handling.
• As long as the decimal value is in the Server 
cache, the cached value is returned, even if it 
exceeds the scale defined for the database 
column.
• The Change Attribute service requires 
configuration to support the Decimal data type. 
This is because the history table’s column must 
match the decimal type specification of the field 
that is to be changed.
[Decimal]
Fixed Fixed number of decimal and whole number positions. 
The fixed data type is used to store a fixed point number 
as a floating point number in the database. A floating 
point value is an approximation of a fixed point value. 
Note that a Decimal type is available and should be used 
to store an exact numeric value with defined precision 
and scale.
[Fixed], Cost, Percentage, 
Qty, Qty2, Yield
Float Floating Point Number [Float], Duration, Id

properties until they are overridden. This table lists the field properties and the data types that have these 
properties. 
Property Definition Data 
Types
EnumerationSeries of elements that can be based on one of any basic data type. It is

value.
All data 
types 
except 
Object
Precision Number of digits allowed in a number. Precision is currently supported 
only for the Decimal data type.
Decimal, 
Fixed, 
Float, 
Integer
Scale Number of digits to the right of the decimal in a float field. For example, 
the number 123.45 has a precision of 5 and a scale of 2. Scale is 
currently supported only for the Decimal data type.
Decimal, 
Fixed, 
Float
Maximum 
length
Field length by number of characters for string fields. For example, if you 
design a field to have a maximum length of 5 for five characters and a 
user types six characters when defining instances in a client application, 
the system returns an error message. 
String

Note: If you change the data type, the field type you are defining is saved under the new data 
type. Designer additionally instructs you on how to make changes to the database 
column, in case the field is associated with a database column.
7. Set the other attributes for the field type.
Note: If you specify a maximum length for string fields, Designer additionally instructs you on 
how to change database column size, in case the field is associated with a database 
column.
8. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.
How to Modify Field Type Properties
If you modify an existing field property, it is automatically applied. For example, the field type for Revision 
falls within the String data type category and has a 15-character string length. If your revision data 
element requires a 3-character string, you can modify the field type by changing the property of the 15-
character string to a 3-character string within the field type definition.
Follow these steps to modify field type properties in Designer:
1. Click Fields. The Field Type Definitions window appears.
2. Expand the data type category that contains the field type definition.
3. Select the field type definition to be modified.
4. Change the properties. 
Important:The field type information is used when creating a column definition. Once the 
column is defined, changes to the field type do not affect the column. You need 
to manually update the column definition as appropriate.
5. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.
How to Update the Column Definition
Follow these steps to update the column definition:
1. Select the field type.
2. Click the WhereUsed tab.
3. Double-click the name of the column to be modified to open the DB Column Properties window.
4. Modify the column properties. 
Note: Refer to “Tables”  for information about using tables and table columns.
5. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.

Chapter7:Fields
CDO Fields
The field types and data types found in the Fields window are used as attributes of CDO fields. CDO fields 
are found in the Fields tab on the CDO Definition window. This topic discusses:
• Adding a CDO Field
• Selecting the Correct Data Type and Field Type
• Removing Fields
When Adding a CDO Field
Adding a field to a CDO is necessary when customizing Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR. Adding a 
CDO field is different than adding a field type. 
When you add a CDO field, specify the field’s singular data type and field type. Once the field is added, it 
becomes very specific to the CDO to which it is added. 
Do not name two services with the same caption when adding a CDO field. Designer allows you to name 
two services with the same caption, however you cannot indicate which service you are executing.
Adding a CDO field does not affect field type definitions. However, when a CDO is added to the  class 
library, it is added as a Data Type Object field.
Workspace prefixes are automatically inserted before the name of an item when adding a new item, if a 
prefix has been assigned to the highest active workspace. Workspace prefixes  indicate which workspace 
Designer is applying changes to.  For example, the prefix "C200"  would be associated to the workspace 
with a prefix set as "C200"  in Management Studio. It is recommended you use the prefix supplied by  
Designer when adding new items.
You can assign or modify workspace prefixes in Management Studio.  Refer to the Opcenter Execution 
Medical Device and Diagnostics System Administration Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core System 
Administration Guide for information.
Note: The base application workspace (csi) cannot have a workspace prefix assigned to it.
7-12 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 185 ===
Chapter7:Fields
This image shows an example of the Add a New CDO Field window. 
Rules
These are the user-defined field rules: 
• Fields added to CDOs are inherited by all its subclasses. The more subclasses an object has, the 
longer the process can take.
• You can drop default and user-defined CDO fields as controls on Portal forms. 
• If you add fields to your ParametricData subclass, drop the fields as controls onto the 
Parametric Data Control form in the Portal. 
• If you add user-defined fields in a Service CDO, the fields are loaded on the UserDefined 
control of the Portal.
• Lists, subentities, and subentity lists need a custom UI. This is done with the Portal.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 7-13

Chapter7:Fields
• Do not assign the same field usage to two object fields in the same CDO. 
• Refer to "Valid Characters and Reserved Words" for additional restrictions on field naming.
Selecting the Correct Data Type and Field Type
Choosing the correct data type and field type for a field is important to match the needs of your data 
element. Use the following guidelines when choosing a data type and field type:
• Base your selection of a data type and field type on the description and intended use. The 
descriptions of the data types are shown in the Data Type and Field Type table. The descriptions 
of the field types are provided in the Field Type Properties table. 
• Review the properties of the field type to ensure that the properties match your data element 
requirements. 
How to Add a CDO Field 
Follow these steps to add a CDO field in Designer:
1. Click CDOs. The CDO Definition window appears.
2. Select the user-defined CDO subclass that you are adding the fields to.
3. Select the Fields tab.
4. Click Add at the bottom of the Fields tab. The Add a New CDO Field window appears.
5. Select a data type from the Data Type list. Refer to "Selecting the Correct Data Type and Field 
Type" for information on selecting data types and field types.
Note: For persistent objects, a table is created for the list field in the database if you select the 
Field is a list check box.
6. Select a field type from Field Type.
7. Enter a name for the new field, avoiding illegal characters and reserved words. 
Important:The name of the CDO can be up to 30 characters in length, and cannot contain 
spaces, illegal characters, or reserved words. Refer to "Valid Characters and 
Reserved Words."
8. Select an existing label to use as a caption from the Caption list.
Or
Enter a label. If you add your own, the new label is added to the user labels library under the 
OtherField category.
9. Enter a description in the Description field.
10. Select the Add Field to Associated Service Objects check box. This step adds the same field to 
associated service objects. For example, if you are adding a field to an Employee subclass, the 
same field is added to the corresponding EmployeeChanges subclass.
11. Click Create. The CDO Field Properties window appears.
12. Confirm or modify the field properties. 
13. Click OK.

How to Remove Fields
You can remove user-defined fields, but not default fields. Follow these steps to remove CDO fields in 
Designer:
1. Click CDOs. The CDO Definition window appears.
2. Select the CDO subclass to remove a field.
3. Select the Fields tab.
4. Select the user-defined field to be deleted.
The Remove button is disabled if you selected a default field, or a user-defined field that is 
mapped or has overrides.
5. Clear any field overrides and remove field maps. Refer to "Creating Field Overrides" and "Viewing 
Field Maps."
6. Click Remove. A dialog box prompts you to confirm the deletion of the CDO field.
7. Click Yes. The field is removed. If the field has a database column, that column is also deleted.
8. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.

Expressions Tab Field Definitions
This table defines the fields on the Expressions tab.
Field Definition
Current Value Expression (CVE) Current Value Expressions are used to dynamically determine the 
value of a field in a CDO. The intent of a CVE is to allow the field to 
determine a value at run time.
Default Value (DV) Default value is the value that you designate the field will take in the 
absence of any other specified value.
Note: Siemens recommends that you used default values only for 
shop floor transactions.
7-24 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 197 ===
Chapter7:Fields
Field Definition
Default Value Expression (DVE) Default value expression is used to dynamically determine the 
default value.
Note: Siemens recommends that you used default value expressions 
only for shop floor transactions.
Supported expressions
Only CVEs and DVEs support expressions; the DV does not. When initializing an object, the system 
attempts to get a field’s value, if persistent, from the database. 
Note: CVE is not evaluated at the time of initialization. CVE is evaluated only at the time field value is 
requested.
On a request to get the field value, one of the following values is returned. These are executed in the 
following order:
1. Instance data, the value that was set
2. Value returned by OnGetValue event, if present
3. Value returned by the CVE, if present
The result of this processing, which can still be empty, is set to the field if it evaluated to some value. 
Getting the value returns whatever value the field is set to.
There is a call that allows to get the default value, csigetdefaultvalue.
Expressions are used to dynamically determine the value of a field in a CDO, whether it is a Current Value 
Expression or a Default Value Expression. The syntax is similar; the difference is that the current value 
expression can contain a reference to the current instance data value of a field.
A field expression-a CVE or a DVE-refers to more than one expression listed in sequence, separated by 
semicolons. The evaluation sequence of a field expression starts with the first expression, proceeding to 
the last. The evaluation stops when a field expression produces a value. The reserved word is entered first, 
followed by a semicolon. The current reserved word for a field expression is:
This_Value;
This_Value uses the value that the user has entered in the field.
An expression represents one value but might have more than one field name separated by periods within 
the expression. The convention for entering a field expression for a field is:
FieldName(ObjectReference).FieldName(ObjectReference).FieldName(any type)
Where:
FieldName(ObjectReference) is a field in a CDO of type OID, where the field refers to a CDO. 
and:
FieldName(type any) is a field in a CDO of any type.
Example 1
This_Value;Container.Workflow.FirstStep
This expression instructs the application to use the value the user has entered; if nothing was entered, this 
expression returns the Instance ID in the FirstStep field contained in the Workflow belonging to the 
Container.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 7-25

=== PDF PAGE 198 ===
Chapter7:Fields
Example 2
This_Value;Details.Container.Operation;0;
The evaluation for this field expression is as follows:
1. This_Value retrieves the value entered by the user. If this does not yield a value, then;
2. Details.Container.Operation retrieves the value from the Operation field in the Container CDO 
within the Details CDO. If that CVE does not yield a value, then;
3. The value is set to 0, NULL, or an empty string, depending upon the field type.
Example 3
This_Value;ProductFamily.Workflow
The evaluation for this field expression is as follows:
1. This_Value is used in the CVE to refer to the current instance data value of a field.
2. ProductFamily.Workflow retrieves the value from the Workflow field in the Product Family CDO.
Using the Expression Builder
The Expression Builder relieves you of having to type your CVEs or DVEs by displaying a list of CDOs and 
fields in a window. You select the CDO and field, and the Builder automatically enters the appropriate 
delimiters.
7-26 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

the end of a complete expression, and start at step 2 in this list.

Chapter7:Fields
Viewing Field Selection Values
Fields can be configured to offer a list of values, obtained through an Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR 
selection values query, from which a user can select a value. In the Selection Values tab, you can choose 
from the most commonly used options for generating selection values for a field. Opcenter EX MDD and 
Opcenter EX CR automatically attach the corresponding Selection Values CLF to the field. 
If the field already has a Selection Values CLF attached to it, that CLF name will appear in the list of 
Selection Values options.
Selection values are set in the Service object or the Changes object. There are two groups of Selection 
Values:
• Basic
Basic Selection Values are used with ServerLink, the InSite COM Client. They are maintained for 
backward compatibility only to give time for current users to redefine their selection values using 
the Extended Selection Values.  Use the Extended Selection Values For new definitions in 
Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR.
• Extended
For fields with selection values defined, extended selection values is the default in the Selection 
Values tab. Many, but not all, query names for extended selection values start with 
SelectionValuesEx. Extended Selection Values use the XML Application Server for processing and 
are not supported by ServerLink.
Five options for determining selection values are available:
• Static Selection Values
• Query Selection Values
• List Field Selection Values
• Enumeration Selection Values
Note: Extended Selection Values default to  “Static” when adding an NDO, RO, or enumeration field to 
a Modeling changes CDO or Shop Floor Service CDO.
Notice that this field, TextVariableChanges.VariableUsage, is a CDO field, and already has its selection 
values determined by the Enumeration method. Its key values (for storage purposes) and its display strings 
are already defined. You can change any display string by retyping that field in this window, with some 
fields containing entered literals (no quotes needed) and others containing field names.
• CLF Selection Values
The Selection Values options available through this window are explained in the following 
sections. 
Static Selection Values
This topic explains how to use the Static Selection Values option to display all the instances of objects that 
have the same Field Type. The Static option does not allow you to change the Selection Values Query. It 
uses the Selection Values definition of the field’s type.
7-32 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 205 ===
Chapter7:Fields
When you add a CDO, it is also added as a field type within the Object data type. When you set a Selection 
Value for the CDO in the CDO Definition window, it is this selection value that becomes the selection value 
of that field type.
The entry in the Selection Values Query field changes according to the field type. 
This image shows an example of a field with selection values using the Static Selection Values option.

SelectionValuesEx_RDO Retrieves the selection values of a revisioned object.
SelectionValuesEx_Resource Retrieves a list of all resources.
SelectionValuesEx_SplitCandidates Retrieves a list of child containers eligible to be split 
from the specified container.
Refer to “Queries” for information about defining queries. 
List Field Selection Values
List Field selection values are used to retrieve a list of values for a list field. Either Opcenter EX MDD or 
Opcenter EX CR uses CLFs to retrieve the list and does not have a default example of a field using the List 
Field option. The following procedure explains how to set up selection values based on a specified list 
field. The example uses a user-defined field that references a list of named data objects.
How to Use the ListField Extended Selection Values Option
Follow these steps to use the ListField Extended Selection Values option in Designer:
1. Click CDOs. The CDO Definition window appears.
2. Select the CDO for whose list field you want to set selection values.
3. Select the Fields tab if it is not already selected.
4. Select the list field and click Properties. The CDO Field Properties window appears.
5. Select the Selection Values tab.
6. Select ListField: selection values are based on a specified list field from the list.
7. Type an expression in the field labeled Field expression for the list field.
Or
8. Click Build to open the Expression Builder. Refer to "Unified Expressions" for information.
9. Select a field from the list under Available Fields, and then click Add to add additional 
information on each object on the list. The field is added to Fields to include.
10. Enter the expression in the Available Fields to add a field expression.
Or
11. Click Build to open the Expression Builder.
12. Click Add to add the expression to Fields to include.
Note: If the referenced object is an NDO, the fields to include are Name and InstanceID. If the 
object is an RO. the fields are Name, Revision, RevOfRcd, InstanceID, and Status.
13. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.

Viewing and Modifying Field Events
Field events are events that are attached to a CDO field. Refer to “Events and Methods” for information 
about viewing events.
How to View Field Events
Follow these steps to view field events:
1. Open the Designer toolbar.
2. Click CDOs. The CDO Definition window appears.
3. Double-click any CDO in the list.
4. Select the Fields tab. The CDO Fields window appears, displaying the fields unique to this CDO.
5. Select Include Inherited fields to view fields inherited from the base class. 
6. Select the field whose events you want to view.
7. Click the Properties button on this Fields window. The CDO Field Properties window opens. 
8. Click the Events tab. Any events attached to the field are displayed:
9. Select Include Inherited to view events inherited from the parent class if the field you selected is 
inherited. The inherited events associated with the selected field appear.
10. Select Cancel to close the Field Events window.
Modifying Field Events
You can attach CLFs to field events and detach user-defined CLFs. You cannot attach High Performance 
CLFs to field events. Refer to “Configurable Logic Flows”  for information about using CLFs.

9. Select Include inherited to view events inherited from the base class. The events associated with 
the selected field appear.
10. Select the event to which you want to attach a CLF. The Attach CLF button is enabled.
11. Click Attach CLF. The Find Definition window opens, displaying the library of available CLFs:

Chapter7:Fields
Creating Field Overrides
Typically, a CDO subclass uses the inherited fields of its superclass. Overriding customizes a specific 
property of the inherited field within the CDO subclass. Overrides can be done only on inherited fields. 
Changes that are made in the override apply only to the selected CDO and any subclasses of the selected 
CDO. An overridden field value can range from a change in description to a change in object references 
and value expressions. 
This diagram shows the Override Relationship from Superclass to Subclass CDO.
The following guidelines apply to overrides:
• You can change the data type in Designer. Your changes will not affect the database.  
• If the data type is a CDO, you can override it by selecting a different CDO to reference. Note that 
the field type becomes the name of the referenced CDO but the field Name is not affected.
• A field with an overridden value has a  background color  in the Designer window to indicate the 
color associated with the workspace in which it was overridden.
• Making changes to field properties does not update the associated column in the Opcenter EX 
MDD or Opcenter EX CR table that stores that field. You must select the field’s Where Used tab to 
view the tables that use this field. Then make the same property change to all the affected tables. 
Refer to "Tables" for information on accessing the tables.
• If the field has a corresponding field in a maintenance transaction, you are given a default option 
to make the same changes in the corresponding field in the associated maintenance transaction.
• Using a larger number of overrides can result in the allocation of more GDI objects than the 
Windows operating system allows by default per process. You can edit the value of the following 
registry key to increase the maximum value in order to handle the required GDI objects:
HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows NT\
CurrentVersion\Windows\GDIProcessHandleQuota 
The default value is 10,000. The accepted range is 256 to 65,536.
7-42 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 215 ===
Chapter7:Fields
How to Create a Field Override
Follow these steps to create a field override:
1. Open the Designer toolbar.
2. Click CDOs. The CDO Definition window appears.
3. Select a CDO in the CDO Definition window and click the Fields tab.
4. Select Include inherited fields. 
5. Select the field for which you will create an override.
6. Click Properties. A dialog box prompts you to confirm the creation of an override.
7. Click Yes. The CDO Field Properties window appears.
Note: If you click No, you will override the field in the ancestor CDO where the field was 
originally defined, and that override will be inherited by all subclasses of the ancestor 
CDO.
8. Change any field properties you want to modify according to your business requirements. The 
background color of overridden fields turns to  the color associated with the workspace in which 
it was overridden.
9. Click OK.
10. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.

Chapter7:Fields
Defining List Fields
A field is defined as a list field when you check Field is a list on the Add a New CDO Field window. Storing a 
list of objects can be done either statically or dynamically for a list of objects within data CDOs.
Setting Values
A list of objects is represented by a list of object instance IDs. This list can either be stored in a separate 
table or dynamically built if the relationship between the objects already exists.
Dynamic Retrieval
Ideally, you want to use dynamic retrieval because this list does not require a separate table for the list. 
The advantage of not having a separate table is to save database space. 
The object containing the list field references an object that contains a list of objects. There is a single field 
on which to sort for this type of retrieval. This diagram shows that relationship.
How to View the Settings for Dynamic Retrieval
Follow these steps to view the settings for dynamic retrieval:
Important:The list field must be of data type Object. 
1. Select a CDO in the CDO Definition window, and then select the Fields tab.
2. Select the list field for which you need to retrieve entries dynamically.
This example uses the Products field for the ProductFamily CDO.
3. Click Properties  at the bottom of the Fields tab. The CDO Field Properties window appears.
7-44 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 217 ===
Chapter7:Fields
4. Click the  General tab. The General tab appears.
Note: Field is a list is selected and cannot be changed. This is appropriate for fields configured 
for dynamic list retrieval. List entries are retrieved dynamically is selected. Again, this is 
required.
The tab has three fields with lists at the bottom:
• Field from <CDO> to match when building list
This is the name of the field in the CDO (Product in this case, because that is the field type) 
that is the basis for building a list field. In this example, the ProductFamily field of the 
Product CDO is displayed.
• Field from <CDO> to match when building list
This is the field that resides within the base object (ProductFamily in this case) that you want 
to match with the field in the list object. This is the matching criterion and the criterion 
defines the relationship between the two objects. In this example, InstanceID of 
ProductFamily from is displayed.
• Field from <CDO> to use when sequencing list
This is the field that resides within the base object (Product in this case) that you want to 
use for sorting purposes. In this example, ProductBase.Name of Product is displayed. Product 
is a revisioned object and this is its Base is used. If this were a list for named data objects, it 
is instance ID.
Note: The <CDO> in the captions for the three  list boxes change, depending on the 
selection. 
5. Click OK.
Static List Retrieval
A static retrieval of the list entry requires a new table. The new table is used to maintain and store the list 
entries that either must be managed or maintained (for example, to preserve the order in a list). This 
might be necessary if:
• You need a list to manage the order of objects. 
• A list has a many-to-many relationship.
• A list contains a list of data values (as opposed to a list of references to existing objects).
How to Set the Values for Static Retrieval
Follow these steps to set the values for static retrieval:
1. Select a CDO in the CDO Definition window, and then select the Fields tab.
2. Select the list field for which you need to retrieve entries statically.
3. Click Properties  to display the CDO Field Properties window.
4. Click the General tab. The option Field is a list is selected.
5. Click Create a New Table. A default table name and a default column name appear. 
6. Rename the table and column to something meaningful.

Chapter7:Fields
Viewing Field Maps
A map is a link between two CDOs that allows field values to be passed from the source CDO field to the 
target CDO field. You can map a field by extending a CDO map or by adding your own.
By extending a map, you start with an existing map within Opcenter EX MDD or Opcenter EX CR and 
specify a subclass of the currently mapped CDO. Once the map is extended, you can map any of the field 
values from the source to the target fields.
By adding a map, your source CDO is the one selected in the CDO Definition window. The target CDO will 
be the one you select when you add the map. Once the map is added, you can map any of the field values 
from the source to the target fields.
Note: When you add fields to Shop Floor services, you will typically add the same fields to the history 
details CDO and map them. This ensures that the values of these fields are written to the his-
tory when the service is executed.
How to View Existing Maps for a CDO
Maps for a CDO are grouped according to direction, Map from or Map to the CDO. Follow these steps to 
view existing maps for a CDO in Designer:
1. Select the CDO. For example, MoveTxn.
2. Select the Maps tab. The Maps tab opens.
Note: Map from is selected. This view shows that the direction of information mapping starts 
from the current CDO.
3. Click Map to. The Maps window changes to show different maps.
Note: If any map definitions exist in this view, the direction of information mapping will be 
from other CDOs to the current CDO.
How to Map a Field by Extending an Existing Map
Follow these steps to map a field by extending an existing maps in Designer:
1. Click the CDOs button. The CDO Definition window appears.
2. Double-click a CDO in the left pane that you want to extend a map to.
3. Select the Maps tab. The Maps tab appears.
4. Select the map to extend, for example, Container_to_StartHistoryDetail, in the Maps defined 
in Ancestors CDOs pane.
5. Click Extend. The Find definition window appears.
6. Select a CDO to represent your new target CDO.
7. Click Select. The extended map appears in Map definitions.
8. Select the new map in Map definitions.
9. Click Details located below Map definitions. (There are two Details buttons. Do not click the 
Details button below Maps defined in Ancestor CDOs.) The CDO Field Map window appears.

How to Remove a Map
You cannot remove default maps or inherited ones. You cannot remove user-defined maps that have been 
extended by a subclass of the CDO. 
If a CLF was created to call the map to be removed, this CLF must be deleted or modified to no longer 
reference the map before the map can be removed. To identify where maps are used in CLFs refer to "How 
to View References to a Map."
Follow these steps to remove a map:
1. Open the Designer toolbar.
2. Click the CDOs button. The CDO Definition window appears.
3. Double-click the CDO in the left pane that you are removing a map.
4. Select the  Maps tab.
5. Select the map to be removed in Map definitions.
6. Click Remove. A dialog box prompts you to confirm the map removal from the CDO definition.
7. Click Yes.
8. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.
How to Clear Field Maps
Clearing field maps does not remove the field. You are simply removing the mapping relationship.
Note: The process of clearing a field map is specific to the currently selected map.
Follow these steps to clear field maps in Designer:
1. Click the CDOs button. The CDO Definition window appears.
2. Double-click the CDO in the left pane that you are removing the mapped field.
3. Select the Maps tab.
4. Select the map in Map definitions from which to remove the mapped field.
5. Click Details located below Map definitions. (There are two Details buttons. Do not click the 
Details button below Maps defined in Ancestor CDOs.) The CDO Field Map window opens.
6. Select the Fields tab if it is not already selected.
7. Select the mapped source (from) field.
8. Select the mapped target (to) field.
9. Click Clear mapping. The map definition is removed for that field.
10. Repeat steps 7-9 for each desired field.
11. Click the [X] located in the upper, right corner of the window to save your removed field maps.
12. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.

Chapter7:Fields
Encrypting and Masking Fields
You can encrypt field values before transmitting data. Encryption is useful when you transmit user 
passwords, WIP messages, Electronic Signatures, or other information, as sensitive information is secure 
even in troubleshooting situations. Field encryption impacts:
• XML transaction documents
• Transaction trace logs
• DBMON window
When Encrypting Fields
Using Password under Field Usage on the Options tab, you can encrypt fields; however, only one field for 
each CDO can be encrypted. Refer to “Options Tab Field Definitions” for information about the Options 
tab’s fields. 
How to Encrypt a Field
Follow these steps to encrypt a field: 
1. Click the CDOs button. The CDO Definition window appears.
2. Select the CDO in the left pane.
3. Select a field to encrypt from the Field name list, and click the Properties button. The CDO Field 
Properties window appears.
Important:You can only encrypt a field where it is initially defined, not in a subclass, for 
example, override. 
4. Click the Options tab.
5. Select Write-Only in the Accessibility area.
Or
6. Select Read/Write in the Accessibility area.
7. Select Password from the Field Usage list.
8. Click OK.
9. Apply the Designer changes.
Note: Using the Field Directory or Toolbox, you can add encrypted fields to the form.
10. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.
Masking Fields
You can mask fields in the Portal, and bullets will display when you enter values in a field. If an object that 
includes a masked field is retrieved or displayed:
• The field appears blank.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide 7-53

=== PDF PAGE 226 ===
Chapter7:Fields
• If a new value is entered, it is encrypted and sent as part of the request data for the transaction.
• If no value is entered, the field is not sent as part of the request data for the transaction and the 
current value is retained.
• If one or more spaces are entered, the field is sent as part of the request data for the transaction 
and has a null value in the database.
When Masking Fields
You can mask fields by selecting Password under Field type on the General tab. Refer to “CDO Field 
Properties” for information about the General tab’s fields. 
How to Mask a Field
Follow these steps to mask a field:
1. Click the CDOs button. The CDO Definition window appears.
2. Select the CDO in the left pane. 
3. Select a field to mask from the Field name list, and then click the Properties button. The CDO 
Field Properties window appears.
4. Click the General tab.
5. Select String from the Data Type list, and then select Password from the Field type list.
6. Click OK.
7. Apply Designer changes.
Note: Using the Field Directory or Toolbox, you can add masked fields to the form.
8. Select Save from the File menu. The application saves your changes.
