Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide B-3

=== PDF PAGE 336 ===
AppendixB:UnifiedExpressions
Whitespace characters in expressions
Whitespaces are allowed and ignored anywhere between tokens inside expressions. Token examples are 
Field Names, Variable Names, keywords, operators like “>=”, and so on. Tokens must not contain 
whitespaces. The whitespaces inside string literals are preserved as is.
Case sensitivity
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR expressions are case insensitive. Nevertheless, it is recommended 
to use preferred spelling for variables, fields, and keywords. For instance, use “This_Value” instead of “this_
value” or use “Transaction::__Return” instead of “transaction::__RETURN”.
Arithmetic expressions
Arithmetic expression semantics depends on the operand types. For integer operands integer arithmetic is 
applied. If an operation has both integer operand and an operand with a decimal point, then decimal point 
arithmetic is applied. For example, 1/4 evaluates to 0, but 1/4. (note the decimal point after 1/4) evaluates 
to .25.
Text variable substitution processing
The labels supported by Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR may contain text variables that are used for 
error detail information, history inquiry messages, and so on. The variables are substituted either implicitly 
while getting a field value of a field having the Process text variables flag set (for instance, 
Service.CompletionMsg), or explicitly by the ExpandMessage function (for instance, 
HistoryMainline.DisplayName). In the former case, since the label designator is actually stored as a field 
value, the text variable syntax is used. An example is #Label::CompletionMoveIn is used instead of 
Label::CompletionMoveIn.. 
Text variable processing is considered as an optional step in the expression evaluation process.  Refer to 
“Labels With Text Variables”  for information.
Strings
Strings must be in double quotes.
Numbers
Numbers with decimal point are processed using decimal data type.
Operations Not Supported
Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR expressions do not support the following operations:
• Multiplicative (%)
• Bitwise and, xor, or  
B-4 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

B-22 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 355 ===
AppendixB:UnifiedExpressions
Implicit Conversion
This graphic illustrates valid type conversions in Opcenter EX MDD and Opcenter EX CR. Opcenter EX MDD 
and Opcenter EX CR implicitly convert data types in expressions, parameters, and fields when there is a 
type mismatch. 
The type conversion rules include:
• Boolean does not convert to any other data type.
• TimeStamp converts to Boolean.
• Float converts to Boolean.
• String converts to Boolean.
• Object converts to Boolean.
• Decimal converts to Boolean.
• Fixed converts to Decimal or Boolean.
Integer converts to Fixed, Decimal, Float, or Boolean. For example, if a Qty field is of type Fixed, then Qty/4 
is not calculated using integer arithmetic: Literal (string) 4 is promoted to fixed type and fixed arithmetic is 
applied.
For an expression like Qty/4.0, Qty is promoted to Decimal type since all literals with floating point are 
represented as values of type "decimal." Then decimal arithmetic is applied.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide B-23

Appendix C: Utilities
Introduction
This appendix contains information on the external utilities Siemens provides for managing your 
metadata.
In This Appendix
This appendix contains this topic:
• Running the Metadata Export Utility
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide C-1

=== PDF PAGE 358 ===
AppendixC:Utilities
Running the Metadata Export Utility
Siemens provides the Metadata Export utility for viewing the differences between your base metadata 
database (MDB) and a modified MDB. The utility compares the two databases and allows you to selectively 
export the differences (delta) to an XML file. The utility also provides the ability to generate a report 
detailing the differences between the two databases.
When  Running the Metadata Export Utility
The Metadata Export utility appears as a window containing a series of three screens that guide the export 
and report generation process. Each screen displays a screen number in the upper right corner of the 
window to help track your progress.
These screens appear in the utility in the following order:
• Select Files to Compare
• Select Changes to Output
• Select Output Options
You can navigate from one screen to the next in the utility by clicking the Next button. You can return to a 
previous screen by clicking the Previous button. Clicking Cancel will close the Metadata Export window 
and an export will not be performed and a metadata report will not be generated.
The About Metadata Export pop-up appears when you select About from the Help menu and displays 
product and version information about the Metadata Export utility. You can close the About Metadata 
Export pop-up by clicking the Close button.
The utility displays an error message when an error occurs during the export process. The error message 
contains information on where in the process the utility encountered the error and what objects it cannot 
compile. Errors are logged in the Event Viewer and in a log file containing detailed information explaining 
why the process failed. Log files are saved at the file path C:\Program Files (x86)\Camstar\Logs by default.
Select Files to Compare Screen
You must specify a base MDB and a modified MDB to compare on the Select Files to Compare screen. The 
same MDB file cannot be used for both the base and modified MDB. The utility displays the highest active 
workspace for each database, and the workspaces must be the same in order to proceed. The utility only 
compares databases with the same workspace. The last MDBs that were previously specified when using 
the utility will appear in these fields by default.
Note: Workspaces are activated  in Management Studio. Refer to the Opcenter Execution Medical 
Device and Diagnostics System Administration Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core System 
Administration Guide for information. 
The utility begins compiling a database after you specify a database in  the Base MDB or Modified 
MDB fields. The text "Compiling" appears next to the field and the text "Compiled" then appears when the 
utility successfully finishes compiling the database. The utility enables the Next button when both 
databases have finished compiling.
The utility compares the base and modified MDBs when you click the Next button on the Select Files to 
Compare screen. A Status pop-up appears and provides text updates during the comparison  process. The 
Status pop-up closes when the process  finishes.
C-2 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 359 ===
AppendixC:Utilities
Select Changes to Output Screen
The Select Changes to Output screen displays a list of definitions in a series of trees arranged by object 
type for the modified objects found during the MDB comparison. 
The following object types appear on this screen:
• CDOs 
• CLFs
• Fields
• Database Tables
• Functions
• Queries
• Labels
• Features
Note: IDs will not be in sync with your target environment when performing an export that includes 
entitled features. Siemens recommends that you use the Replace Workspace Data utility to 
ensure IDs are updated as part of the replacement process. Refer to the  for information on the 
Replace Workspace Data utility.Opcenter Execution Medical Device and Diagnostics Installation 
Guide or the Opcenter Execution Core Installation Guide.
You can expand a tree and select specific object definitions to include in the export or metadata report. 
Additional object definition information appears in the right pane of the screen when you select an object 
definition. You can select all the available object definitions by clicking the Select All button. You can also 
click the Expand All button to expand all the trees to view the complete list of modified object definitions.
Note: A warning message appears when the Expand All button is clicked to notify you that the pro-
cess of expanding all the trees may take a few minutes if there is a large number of modified 
objects. Click OK to close the warning message.
You can search for specific objects by entering the object's name in the Search field and clicking the Search 
button.
A warning message stating that no changes have been selected for export appears if you click Next 
without selecting at least one definition. Click OK to close the warning message and then select at least 
one definition.
Select Output Options Screen
The Select Output Options screen allows you to control whether the export tool generates:
• A metadata export XML file,
• A metadata export report (HTML or DOC), or
• Both.
Select the:
• Export File check box to generate the metadata export XML file.
• Report File check box to generate the metadata report file.
• Include IDs for Feature Promotion check box to include the metadata IDs in the metadata export 
XML file.    
Selecting either the Export File or Report File check boxes enables the associated text box and file selection 
functionality so you can specify the output file and path.
Release2504+Rev.1 DesignerUserGuide C-3

=== PDF PAGE 360 ===
AppendixC:Utilities
The metadata export tool enables the Finish button after you specify the desired output, file, and file 
location. Clicking the Finish button begins the file generation. A Status pop-up appears and displays a 
success message when file generation is complete.
When Generating Export and Metadata Report Files
The utility saves exports in an XML format and metadata reports in an HTML format by default. The utility 
supports both HTML and DOC formats for metadata reports. 
The default path for exported XML files and metadata report files is  C:\Users\<user folder>\Desktop (for 
example, C:\Users\msmith\Desktop). 
The default file name for XML files is  the modified file name followed by the workspace and the word 
"export" all separated by underscores. For example, if the modified file name is ModifiedMDB.mdb and the 
workspace is csi, then the default file name would be ModifiedMDB_csi_export.xml.
The default file name  for generated report files is the modified file name followed by the workspace and 
the phrase "exportReport" all separated by underscores. For example, if the modified file name is 
ModifiedMDB.mdb and the workspace is csi, then the default file name would be ModifiedMDB_csi_
exportReport.html.  
You can specify a different path and file name for exports by clicking Browse for the  Export File field. You 
can specify a different path, file name, and file type for reports by clicking Browse for the Report File field.
Running the Metadata Export Utility from the Command Prompt
You can run the Metadata Export utility  from  the command prompt.  You call the utility using the following 
command:
"MetadataExport [-a] [-b baseFileName] [-m modifiedMDBFileName] [-o outputXMLFileName] [-r 
reportHMTLFileName]"
Each parameter is separated by a single space and starts with a dash. The values you  replace the variables 
with are identical to the values expected by the fields across the utility's screens. Refer to "Camstar 
Metadata Export Window Field Definitions" for information.
This table defines the information you must  replace the variables with in the command line.
Variable Value
-a Exports all differences in command line mode with no UI interaction or 
filtering.
Note: You must include this parameter when running the utility from the 
command prompt.
baseFileName The full path to the base metadata database.
modifiedMDBFileNameThe full path to the modified metadata database. 
outputXMLFileName The full path and file name for the exported XML file.
reportHTMLFileNameThe full path and file name for the generated report.
An error message and help information appear if the utility encounters an error. You can also enter 
"MetadataExport help" and press Enter to display help information. You can review the information and 
then attempt to run the utility by reentering your command and pressing Enter. 
C-4 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

Note: Only the entire MDB can be exported via the command prompt.

How to Run the Camstar Metadata Export Utility
Follow these steps to run the Camstar Metadata Export utility:
1. Go to the Apps view.
2. Click the Metadata Export app. The Camstar Metadata Export window appears. 
3. Click Browse for the Base MDB field. The Open window appears. 
C-6 DesignerUserGuide Release2504+Rev.1

=== PDF PAGE 363 ===
AppendixC:Utilities
4. Navigate to the base MDB and click Open. The Open window closes, the path to the base MDB 
appears in the Base MDB field, the code identifying the highest active workspace for the base 
MDB appears in the Workspace field, and "Compiling" appears next to the Base MDB  field. 
"Compiled" then appears next to the Base MDB field when the database finishes compiling.
5. Click Browse for the Modified MDB field. The Open window appears.
6. Navigate to the modified MDB and click Open. The Open window closes, the path to the 
modified MDB appears in the Modified MDB field, the code identifying the highest active 
workspace for the modified MDB appears in the Workspace field, and   "Compiling" appears next 
to the Modified MDB field. "Compiled" then appears next to the Modified MDB field when the 
database finishes compiling and the utility enables the Next button.
7. Click Next. The Select Changes to Output screen and a Status pop-up appear. The Status pop-up 
displays text updates while the utility compares the two databases. The Status pop-up closes 
when the process  completes.
8. Select modified object definitions as needed and then click Next. The Select Output Options 
screen appears.
9. Do you want to perform  the export, generate the metadata report, or perform both the export 
and generate the metadata report?
If you want to . . . Then . . .
Perform the export a. Select the Export File check box.
b. Click Browse for the Export File field. The Save As 
window appears and displays the default file name 
and location.
c. Specify a different path or file name if necessary.
d. Click Save. The Save As window closes, the path to 
the export file and file name appear in the Export 
File field, and the utility enables the Finish button.
e. Go to step 10.
Generate the metadata 
report
a. Select the Report File check box.
b. Click Browse for the Report File field. The Save As 
window appears and displays the default file name 
and location.
c. Specify a different path, file name, or file type if 
necessary.
d. Click Save. The Save As window closes, the path to 
the metadata report and file name appear in the 
Report File field, and the utility enables the Finish 
button.
e. Go to step 10.
